# The law in its current state as of today ( 2024.02.14. ).

The Parliament
in the interest of protecting the inviolable and inalienable fundamental rights of human beings, as well as the independence, territorial integrity, economy of the country, and the national wealth,
taking into account Hungary's international legal and European Union obligations,
for the purpose of enforcing the exclusive punitive power of the state
enacts the following law:

## 1. §

(1) The criminal liability of the perpetrator may only be established for an act which – with the exception of acts punishable under generally recognized rules of international law – was prescribed by law to be punishable at the time of commission.
(2) No punishment may be imposed or measure applied for the commission of a criminal offense which was not provided for by law at the time of commission – or in the case of application of

## 2. § (2) at the time of adjudication –

## 2. §

(1) A criminal offense shall be adjudicated according to the criminal law in force at the time of its commission, subject to the exceptions set out in paragraphs (2)–(3).
(2) If, according to the new criminal law in force at the time of adjudication of the act, the act is no longer a criminal offense or is to be judged more leniently, the new criminal law shall be applied. *
(3) The new criminal law shall be applied retroactively in the adjudication of an act punishable under generally recognized rules of international law if it was not punishable under Hungarian criminal law at the time of commission.

## 3. § (1) Hungarian criminal law shall be applied to

a) a criminal offense committed in the territory of Hungary,
b) a criminal offense committed on a floating facility flying the Hungarian flag or on an aircraft bearing Hungarian markings located outside the territory of Hungary,
c) an act committed abroad by a Hungarian citizen which constitutes a criminal offense under Hungarian law.
(2) Hungarian criminal law shall also be applied to
a) an act committed abroad by a non-Hungarian citizen if it
aa) constitutes a criminal offense under Hungarian law and is also punishable under the law of the place of commission,
ab) * is a criminal offense against the state – except for espionage against allied armed forces and espionage against the institutions of the European Union – regardless of whether it is punishable under the law of the place of commission,
ac) is a criminal offense defined in Chapter XIII or XIV, or any other criminal offense the prosecution of which is prescribed by an international treaty promulgated by law,
b) an act committed abroad by a non-Hungarian citizen against a Hungarian citizen, a legal person established under Hungarian law, or other entity without legal personality, which is punishable under Hungarian law.
(3) In the cases specified in paragraph (2), criminal proceedings shall be initiated by order of the Chief Prosecutor.

## 4. §

(1) A criminal offense is an act committed intentionally or – if this law also prescribes punishment for negligent commission – negligently, which is dangerous to society and for which this law prescribes the imposition of punishment. *
(2) An act dangerous to society is an activity or omission which violates or endangers the person or rights of others, or Hungary's social, economic, or state order as defined by the Fundamental Law.

## 5. §

A criminal offense is either a felony or a misdemeanor. A felony is an intentionally committed criminal offense for which this law prescribes the imposition of a punishment more severe than two years' imprisonment; all other criminal offenses are misdemeanors.

## 6. §

(1) Concurrence of offenses exists when one or more acts of the perpetrator constitute multiple criminal offenses and they are adjudicated in a single proceeding.
(2) * It is not concurrence of offenses but a continuing criminal offense when the perpetrator commits the same criminal offense multiple times within a short period of time, with a single resolution, against the same victim.
(3) * If multiple or continuous acts of the perpetrator constitute one criminal offense, or if multiple criminal offenses of the perpetrator constitute one criminal offense pursuant to a provision of this law, and charges have been brought against the perpetrator for this criminal offense, any further identical criminal offense committed by the perpetrator after the bringing of charges shall be adjudicated as an independent criminal offense.

## 7. §

A criminal offense is committed intentionally when the perpetrator desires the consequences of his or her act or acquiesces in those consequences.

## 8. §

A criminal offense is committed negligently when the perpetrator foresees the possible consequences of his or her act but recklessly relies on their non-occurrence, or does not foresee the possible consequences of his or her act because he or she fails to exercise the attention or care that can be expected of him or her.

## 9. §

More severe legal consequences attached to a result as a qualifying circumstance of a criminal offense may be applied if the perpetrator is at least negligent with respect to the result.
1

## 0. §

(1) An attempt is punishable when a person commences the commission of an intentional criminal offense but does not complete it.
(2) The penalty prescribed for the completed criminal offense shall be applied to an attempt.
(3) The punishment may be reduced without limitation or may also be waived if the attempt is committed on an unsuitable object, with unsuitable means, or in an unsuitable manner.
(4) An attempt is not punishable if
a) the completion of the criminal offense fails to occur as a result of the person's voluntary withdrawal, or
b) the person voluntarily prevents the occurrence of the result.
(5) If, in the cases specified in paragraph (4), the attempt in itself already constitutes another criminal offense, the perpetrator may be punished for that criminal offense.
1

## 1. §

(1) When specifically prescribed by this law, preparation is punishable when a person, for the purpose of committing a criminal offense, secures the necessary conditions or conditions facilitating it, invites, offers, undertakes to commit it, or agrees to commit it jointly.
(2) Preparation is not punishable if
a) the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense fails to occur as a result of the person's voluntary withdrawal,
b) the person withdraws his or her prior invitation, offer, or undertaking for the purpose of preventing the commission, or endeavors to cause the other participants to withdraw from the commission, provided that the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense fails to occur for any reason, or
c) the person reports the preparation to the authorities before the commencement of the commission of the criminal offense.
(3) * If, in the cases specified in paragraph (2), the preparation in itself already constitutes another criminal offense, the perpetrator may be punished for that criminal offense.
1

## 2. §

A perpetrator is a principal, an indirect principal, and a co-principal (hereinafter together: principals), as well as an instigator and an accomplice (hereinafter together: accessories).
1

## 3. § (1) A principal is a person who fulfills the statutory elements of a criminal offense

(2) An indirect principal is a person who fulfills the statutory elements of an intentional criminal offense through the use of a person who is not punishable for this act due to infancy, morbid mental state, duress or threat, or who is in error.
(3) Co-principals are persons who, being aware of each other's activity, jointly fulfill the statutory elements of an intentional criminal offense.
1

## 4. § (1) An instigator is a person who intentionally induces another to commit a criminal offense

(2) An accomplice is a person who intentionally provides assistance to another in the commission of a criminal offense.
(3) The penalty prescribed for the principals shall also be applied to accessories.
1

## 5. §

The punishability of the perpetrator or the punishability of the act is excluded or limited by:
a) infancy,
b) morbid mental state,
c) duress and threat,
d) error,
e) self-defense,
f) necessity,
g) authorization by law,
h) other grounds specified by law.
1

* A person who has not reached the age of fourteen at the time of committing the punishable act is not punishable, except
a) for homicide [16

1

## 7. §

(1) A person who commits a punishable act in such a pathological state of mental functioning that renders him or her incapable of recognising the consequences of his or her act or of acting in accordance with such recognition is not punishable.
(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if the pathological state of mental functioning limits the perpetrator in recognising the consequences of the crime or in acting in accordance with such recognition.
1

## 8. §

1

## 7. §

shall not apply to a person who commits the crime in a state of intoxication or stupor resulting from his or her own fault.
1

## 9. §

(1) A person who commits a punishable act under the influence of coercion or threat that renders him or her incapable of conduct in accordance with his or her will is not punishable.
(2) The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if the coercion or threat limits the perpetrator of the crime in conduct in accordance with his or her will.
2

## 0. §

(1) The perpetrator is not punishable for a fact of which he or she was not aware at the time of commission.
(2) A person who commits a punishable act in the mistaken belief that it is not dangerous to society and has reasonable grounds for such belief is not punishable.
(3) Mistake does not exclude punishability if it is caused by negligence and this Act provides for the punishment of negligent commission as well.
2

## 1. §

The act of a person who causes harm to an unlawful attacker by means of a defensive device installed for the purpose of preventing an unlawful attack against his or her own person or property or those of others, which is not capable of taking life, is not punishable, provided that the defender has done everything that can be expected in the given situation to ensure that the defensive device installed by him or her does not cause harm.
2

## 2. §

(1) An act that is necessary to repel an unlawful attack directed against or directly threatening one's own person or property or those of another or others or the public interest is not punishable.
(2) An unlawful attack shall be regarded as if it were also directed at taking the life of the defender if
a) it is committed against a person
aa) at night,
ab) armed,
ac) in an armed state, or
ad) in a group,
b) it is an unlawful intrusion into a dwelling
ba) at night,
bb) armed,
bc) in an armed state, or
bd) in a group, or
c) it is an unlawful intrusion armed into an enclosed area belonging to a dwelling.
(3) A person who exceeds the necessary extent of defence out of fright or excusable agitation is not punishable.
(4) The person attacked is not obliged to evade the unlawful attack.
2

## 3. §

(1) The act of a person who saves his or her own person or property or those of another from immediate danger that cannot otherwise be averted, or acts in this way for the protection of the public interest, is not punishable, provided that the act does not cause greater harm than that which he or she sought to avert.
(2) A person who causes greater harm than that which he or she sought to avert because, out of fright or excusable agitation, he or she does not recognise the magnitude of the harm is not punishable.
(3) Necessity cannot be established in favour of a person to whom the causing of the danger is attributable or for whom the assumption of the danger is a duty by virtue of his or her occupation.
2

## 4. §

An act that is permitted or declared unpunishable by law is not punishable.
2

## 5. § Punishability is terminated by

a) the death of the perpetrator,
b) limitation,
c) pardon,
d) active repentance,
e) other grounds specified by law.
2

## 6. §

(1) Punishability – with the exception of those specified in (2)–(3), or in the absence of a different provision of the Act on the exclusion of limitation of certain crimes – is subject to limitation upon the expiry of a period corresponding to the upper limit of the sentencing range, but at least five years.
(2) * Punishability for crimes defined in Chapter XXVII is subject to limitation after twelve years.
(3) * Punishability is not subject to limitation for
a) crimes defined in Chapters XIII and XIV,
b) crimes punishable by life imprisonment, and
c) crimes defined in Chapter XIX punishable more severely than five years' imprisonment if the victim of the crime had not reached the age of eighteen at the time of commission of the crime.
2

## 7. § The starting day of limitation is

a) in the case of a completed crime, the day when the statutory elements are fulfilled,
b) in the case of attempt and preparation, the day when the act constituting them ends,
c) in the case of a crime that is committed solely by the omission of the performance of a duty, the day when the perpetrator could still fulfil his or her duty without the consequence established in this Act,
d) in the case of a crime that consists in the maintenance of an unlawful state, the day when this state ceases.
2

## 8. §

(1) * Limitation is interrupted by a criminal procedural act taken by the court, the prosecutor's office, the investigating authority, or in cases with international aspects, the minister responsible for justice or a foreign authority against the perpetrator on account of the crime. On the day of interruption, the limitation period begins again.
(1a) * If the victim of homicide committed in a state of strong agitation, intentional grievous bodily harm punishable more severely than three years' imprisonment, kidnapping, human trafficking and forced labour, violation of personal liberty, or – with the exception set out in 2

## 6. §

(3) bekezdés c) pontjában – a crime against the freedom of sexual life and sexual morality had not yet reached the age of eighteen at the time of commission of the crime, the period during which he or she does not or would not have reached the age of twenty-one is not included in the limitation period.
(2) * If criminal proceedings are suspended, the duration of the suspension is not included in the limitation period. This provision shall not apply if the criminal proceedings are suspended because the identity of the perpetrator could not be established in the investigation, he or she is at an unknown location, or has become mentally ill, or if the accused at large is at a known location abroad and the criminal proceedings cannot be conducted in his or her absence.
(3) * The period during which criminal proceedings could not be initiated or continued because the immunity based on the right of immunity was not suspended by the person entitled to decide is not included in the limitation period. This provision shall not apply to a crime punishable on private motion in respect of which the prosecution is represented by the private prosecutor.
(4) * In the case of probation, the duration of the probationary period and the duration of reparation work are not included in the limitation period.
2

## 9. §

(1) A person who, before the indictment is filed, confessed to the commission of a misdemeanour or a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property, or against intellectual property rights, and within the framework of a mediation procedure – or prior to it, but approved in an agreement concluded within the framework of the mediation procedure – made reparation for the harm caused by the criminal offence in a manner and to an extent accepted by the victim, shall not be punishable. This provision shall also apply if, among the criminal offences in concurrence, the criminal offence against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property or against intellectual property rights is the determining one.
(2) The punishment may be reduced without limitation if the perpetrator, in the case of criminal offences specified in paragraph (1), confessed to the commission of a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding five years before the indictment is filed, and within the framework of a mediation procedure – or prior to it, but approved in an agreement concluded within the framework of the mediation procedure – made reparation for the harm caused by the criminal offence in a manner and to an extent accepted by the victim. This provision shall also apply if, among the criminal offences in concurrence, the criminal offence against life, bodily integrity and health, against human freedom, against human dignity and certain fundamental rights, traffic-related, against property or against intellectual property rights is the determining one.
(3) Paragraphs (1)–(2) shall not apply if the perpetrator
a) is a multiple or special recidivist,
b) committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
c) his or her criminal offence caused death,
d) * committed the intentional criminal offence during the probationary period of the suspension of imprisonment or after being sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for an intentional criminal offence, before the completion of the execution of the imprisonment, or during probation or conditional prosecutorial suspension, or
e) * previously participated in a mediation procedure for an intentional criminal offence, and as a result paragraph (1) or (2) was applied to him or her, provided that two years have not yet elapsed from the date of the decision not subject to further legal remedy until the commission of the further intentional criminal offence.
3

## 0. § Prosecution is precluded by the absence of

a) a private motion,
b) a complaint.
3

## 1. §

(1) In the cases specified in this Act, the perpetrator of a criminal offence may be punished only upon a private motion.
(2) The victim is entitled to submit the private motion.
(3) * In the cases specified in Section 6

## 9. §

paragraph (5) of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, the private motion may be submitted by the legal representative of the victim. The private motion may also be submitted independently by a victim who is a minor with limited legal capacity, and his or her legal representative is also entitled to do so. In these cases, the guardianship authority is also entitled to submit the private motion. In case of conflict of interest, the provisions of the Civil Code shall apply.
(4) If the victim entitled to submit the private motion dies, his or her relative is entitled to submit the private motion.
(5) A private motion submitted against any perpetrator is effective against all perpetrators.
(6) A private motion may not be withdrawn.
3

## 2. §

In the cases specified in this Act, the perpetrator of a criminal offence may be punished only upon a complaint made by the person entitled to do so.
3

## 3. § (1) Punishments are

a) imprisonment,
b) confinement,
c) community service work,
d) fine,
e) disqualification from occupation,
f) disqualification from driving,
g) expulsion,
h) disqualification from attending sporting events,
i) expulsion from the country.
(2) Disqualification from public affairs is an accessory punishment.
(3) Punishments – with the exceptions set out in paragraphs (5) and (6) – may also be imposed in combination.
(4) * If the lower limit of the sentencing range of the criminal offence does not reach one year of imprisonment, instead of imprisonment, confinement, community service work, fine, disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sporting events or expulsion from the country, or several of these punishments may be imposed.
(5) If this Act provides for a criminal offence to be punished by confinement, instead of or in addition to this punishment, community service work, fine, disqualification from occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sporting events or expulsion from the country, or several of these punishments may be imposed.
(6) The following may not be imposed:
a) confinement or community service work in addition to imprisonment,
b) community service work or fine in addition to expulsion from the country,
c) * fine in addition to life imprisonment.
3

## 4. § Imprisonment is for a definite period or for life

3

## 5. §

(1) If the court imposes imprisonment, it shall order its execution in a prison, correctional facility or penitentiary.
(2) Having regard to the circumstances relevant to the imposition of the punishment, a degree of execution one degree more lenient or one degree more severe than that specified in the Act may be determined. This provision shall not apply to life imprisonment imposed pursuant to Section 4

## 6. §

The shortest duration of imprisonment for a definite period is three months, the longest duration is twenty years; in case of commission in a criminal organisation, as a special or multiple recidivist, or in case of concurrence or cumulative punishment, twenty-five years.
3

## 7. §

(1) The degree of execution of the punishment of imprisonment is prison if it was imposed for a misdemeanour, except if the convicted person is a recidivist.
(2) The degree of execution of the punishment of imprisonment is correctional facility if it was imposed
a) for a felony, or
b) for a misdemeanour and the convicted person is a recidivist.
(3) The degree of execution of the punishment of imprisonment is penitentiary if
a) imprisonment of three years or longer duration was imposed for
aa) a criminal offence specified in Chapter XIII, XIV or XXIV,
ab) a military criminal offence also punishable by life imprisonment,
ac) * act of terrorism [Section 31

## 8. §

(1) * In the case of imposing imprisonment for a definite period, the court shall establish in its decision on the merits the earliest date of conditional release, or – in the cases specified in paragraph (4) – that the possibility of conditional release is excluded.
(2) If the possibility of conditional release is not excluded, its earliest date is the day following the completion of
a) two-thirds of the sentence,
b) three-quarters in the case of a recidivist,
but at least three months.
(3) * In the case of imposing imprisonment not exceeding five years – in cases deserving special consideration – the court may provide in its decision on the merits that the convicted person may be conditionally released after serving half of the sentence. This provision shall not apply if the convicted person is a multiple recidivist.
(4) The following may not be conditionally released:
a) a multiple recidivist, if the imprisonment is to be executed in a penitentiary degree,
b) a violent multiple recidivist,
c) a person who committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
d) a person who was sentenced to imprisonment for an intentional criminal offence committed after being sentenced to imprisonment for a definite period, before the completion of the execution or the termination of enforceability,
e) * a person who was sentenced to imprisonment as a principal for the following criminal offences:
ea) genocide, crime against humanity, apartheid, if committed by intentionally causing death [14

(5) * The following may also not be conditionally released:
a) a person sentenced to imprisonment for the commission of a criminal offence specified in paragraph (4) e)
aa) for preparation,

ac) with the application of unlimited mitigation,

c) for a criminal offence against sexual freedom and sexual morality punishable by imprisonment of eight years or more severe, committed against a person who has not reached the age of eighteen.
(6) * The court may provide in its decision on the merits for the conditional release eligibility of the offender specified in paragraph (5), if, having regard to the circumstances of the commission, the degree of danger to society inherent in the offender's person or other sentencing circumstances, the protection of society and the prevention of the offender from committing a new criminal offence can be achieved by ordering probation supervision and determining a separate rule of conduct.
(7) * For the purposes of paragraph (5) b), relative shall also mean the parent of the offender's child, former spouse, former cohabitant, guardian, ward, legal guardian or ward.
3

## 9. §

(1) * In the case of imprisonment for a definite period, the duration of conditional release is equal to the remaining part of the imprisonment, but at least one year. In the application of 3

## 8. §

(3), the court may provide in its decision on the merits that the duration of conditional release is extended by at least one and at most three years.
(2) If the remaining part of the imprisonment is shorter than one year and its execution has not been ordered, the sentence – after the expiry of the conditional release – shall be deemed to have been completed on the last day of the remaining part.
4

## 0. § (1) * The court shall terminate the conditional release if the convicted person is

a) sentenced to imprisonment during the period of conditional release for a criminal offence committed after the decision on the merits became final, or
b) sentenced to imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during the period of conditional release.
(2) The court may terminate the conditional release if the convicted person is sentenced to a penalty other than those specified in paragraph (1).
(3) In the case of termination of conditional release, the time spent on conditional release shall not be counted towards the imprisonment.
(4) * If during the period of conditional release imprisonment imposed on the convicted person for a criminal offence committed before the earlier decision on the merits became final is to be executed, the execution of the imprisonment interrupts the conditional release, and the court postpones the earliest date of continuation of the conditional release until
a) the date of conditional release granted from the subsequently imposed imprisonment, or
b) – if the possibility of conditional release is excluded in the case of the subsequently imposed imprisonment – the duration of the execution of the imprisonment.
4

## 1. §

(1) * Life imprisonment may be imposed on a person who has reached the age of twenty at the time of committing the criminal offence. This provision also applies to life imprisonment that may be imposed pursuant to 9

## 0. §

(2).
(2) The degree of execution of life imprisonment is penitentiary.
4

## 2. §

* In the case of imposing life imprisonment, the court shall determine in its decision on the merits the earliest date of conditional release, or exclude the possibility of conditional release.
4

## 3. §

(1) If the court does not exclude the possibility of conditional release in the case of imposing life imprisonment, it shall establish its earliest date at a minimum of twenty-five and a maximum of forty years. The earliest date of conditional release shall be determined in years.
(2) In the case of life imprisonment, the duration of conditional release is at least fifteen years.
4

(1) In the case of imposing life imprisonment, the court may exclude the possibility of conditional release only for the following criminal offences:
a) genocide [14

(2) The possibility of conditional release must be excluded if the offender
a) is a violent multiple recidivist, or
b) committed the criminal offence specified in paragraph (1) in a criminal organisation.
4

## 5. §

(1) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment to be executed for a criminal offence committed before being sentenced to life imprisonment, during the execution of the life imprisonment, the court shall postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment to be executed.
(2) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment to be executed for a criminal offence committed before being sentenced to life imprisonment, during conditional release from life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment to be executed.
(3) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during the execution of life imprisonment, during the execution of the life imprisonment, the court shall postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.
(4) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during the execution of life imprisonment, during conditional release from life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.
(5) If the convicted person is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment for a criminal offence committed during conditional release from life imprisonment, the court shall revoke the conditional release and postpone the earliest date of conditional release until the duration of the definite term of imprisonment, but for a period of at least five and at most twenty years.
(6) * If the postponement of the earliest date of conditional release from life imprisonment occurs due to the definite term of imprisonment under paragraphs (1), (2), (4) and (5), the earliest date of conditional release shall be determined taking into account the time of pre-trial detention and criminal supervision credited to the definite term of imprisonment.
(7) * The convicted person may not be conditionally released if he is sentenced to life imprisonment repeatedly. If the previous life imprisonment has not yet been executed, the repeatedly imposed life imprisonment may not be executed.
(8) * The court shall revoke the conditional release if the convicted person is sentenced to life imprisonment repeatedly
a) during conditional release from life imprisonment, or
b) for a criminal offence committed during conditional release from life imprisonment.
4

## 6. §

(1) The term of detention shall be determined in days, its shortest term being five days and its longest term ninety days.
(2) Detention shall be executed in a penal institution.
4

## 7. §

(1) The term of community service work shall be determined in hours, its minimum being forty-eight hours and its maximum three hundred and twelve hours.
(2) * The convicted person shall perform the community service work, unless otherwise provided by law, at least one day per week – on the weekly rest day or in his free time – without remuneration.
(3) *
(4) The person sentenced to community service work is obliged to perform the work assigned to him. The offender may be obliged to perform work which – having regard to his state of health and qualifications – he is foreseeable capable of performing.
4

## 8. §

(1) If the convicted person fails to perform the work assigned to him through his own fault, the community service work or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment.
(2) The imprisonment replacing the community service work shall be executed in a penal institution, in prison grade.
(3) The imprisonment replacing the community service work may be shorter than three months.
4

## 9. §

If the convicted person is sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for a criminal offence committed after being sentenced to community service work, and the community service work sentence has not yet been executed, the community service work or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment so that four hours of community service work corresponds to one day of imprisonment. After the conversion, the remaining community service work corresponds to one day of imprisonment.
5

## 0. §

(1) The fine shall be imposed by determining – having regard to the objective gravity of the criminal offence – the number of day-fines, and – according to the offender's property, income, personal circumstances and way of life – the amount corresponding to one day-fine.
(2) A person who is sentenced to a definite term of imprisonment for a criminal offence committed for the purpose of gaining profit, if he has adequate income or property, shall also be sentenced to a fine.
(3) * The minimum amount of the fine is thirty day-fines, the maximum amount is five hundred and forty day-fines. The amount of one day-fine shall be determined at a minimum of one thousand three hundred forints and a maximum of six hundred and fifty thousand forints.
(4) * The court may order in its decision on the merits – having regard to the offender's property and income circumstances – that the offender may pay the fine in monthly instalments within a maximum of two years.
5

## 1. §

(1) If the convicted person does not pay the fine, or in case of authorisation of payment in instalments fails to pay one monthly instalment, the fine or the unpaid part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment.
(2) If the fine was imposed in addition to imprisonment to be executed, or the execution of the suspended imprisonment was ordered, the grade of execution of the imprisonment replacing the fine shall be governed by the grade of the imprisonment. In other cases, the imprisonment replacing the fine shall be executed in prison grade.
(3) The imprisonment replacing the fine may be shorter than three months.
5

## 2. §

(1) A person may be disqualified from practising an occupation if he commits the criminal offence
a) by violating the rules of his occupation requiring professional qualifications, or
b) intentionally, by using his occupation.
(2) Point a) of paragraph (1) may also be applied to a person who, at the time of committing the criminal offence, did not practise the activity as his occupation, but holds the professional qualifications necessary for practising the occupation the rules of which he violated when committing the criminal offence.
(3) * A person who commits a criminal offence against the freedom of sexual life and sexual morality, if he committed the criminal offence against a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or a person who commits child pornography, shall be disqualified with final effect from practising any occupation or other activity in the framework of which he educates, supervises, cares for, or provides medical treatment to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or is in any other power or influence relationship with such person.
(4) * A person who commits the criminal offence of endangering a minor shall be disqualified from practising any occupation or other activity in the framework of which he educates, supervises, cares for, or provides medical treatment to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or is in any other power or influence relationship with such person. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from practising an occupation may be dispensed with.
(5) * A person who commits the criminal offence of unlawful influencing of the will of voters shall be disqualified from being a responsible person in any civil organisation and a leading officer in a party. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from practising an occupation may be dispensed with.
5

## 3. §

(1) Disqualification from an occupation is for a definite period or permanent.
(2) The shortest duration of disqualification for a definite period is one year, the longest duration is ten years. A person who is unsuitable or unworthy to practice the occupation may be disqualified permanently.
(3) * The duration of disqualification from an occupation begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from an occupation is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release shall be included in the duration of disqualification from an occupation.
(4) The court may, upon request, exempt the disqualified person from permanent disqualification if ten years have elapsed since the disqualification and the disqualified person has become suitable for practicing the occupation, or – if the disqualification was applied due to unworthiness – has become worthy. In the latter case, a person who committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation may not be exempted.
5

## 4. § For the purposes of this subtitle, an occupation also includes where the perpetrator is

a) a member of the body performing the general management of a business organisation or its sole manager,
b) a member of the supervisory board of a business association or cooperative,
c) a member of an individual firm,
d) an individual entrepreneur, or
e) a leading officer of a civil organisation as defined in the Act on civil organisations.
5

## 5. § (1) A person may be disqualified from driving a vehicle if

a) he commits the criminal offence by violating the rules of driving a vehicle requiring a licence, or
b) he uses a vehicle to commit criminal offences.
(2) A person who commits a criminal offence of driving a vehicle while intoxicated or driving a vehicle while under the influence of drugs shall be disqualified from driving a vehicle. In cases deserving special consideration, the mandatory application of disqualification from driving a vehicle may be dispensed with.
(3) Disqualification from driving a vehicle may also apply to a specific type (air, rail, water or road) and category of vehicle.
5

## 6. §

(1) Disqualification from driving a vehicle is for a definite period or permanent.
(2) * The time for which the perpetrator's driving licence was withdrawn at the scene in connection with the criminal offence – prior to his conviction for disqualification from driving a vehicle –, he surrendered it at the authority, or the immediate suspension of the driving licence was registered at the scene in the licence register, shall be included in the duration of disqualification from driving a vehicle.
(3) The shortest duration of disqualification for a definite period is one month, the longest duration is ten years. Disqualification from driving a vehicle shall be determined in months or years, or in years and months.
(4) * The duration of disqualification from driving a vehicle begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from driving a vehicle is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release shall be included in the duration of disqualification from driving a vehicle.
(5) A person who is unsuitable for driving a vehicle may be disqualified permanently. The court may, upon request, exempt the disqualified person from permanent disqualification if ten years have elapsed since the disqualification and the disqualified person has become suitable for driving a vehicle.
5

## 7. §

(1) In cases specified in this Act, a person whose presence there endangers the public interest may be banned from one or more settlements, or from a settlement or a specified part of the country.
(2) The shortest duration of the ban is one year, the longest duration is five years.
(3) * The duration of the ban begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If the ban is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release shall be included in the duration of the ban.
5

## 8. §

(1) The court may disqualify the perpetrator from
a) attending any sporting event organised in the competition system of any sports federation, or
b) entering any sports facility when it serves as the venue for a sporting event organised in the competition system of any sports federation,
for a criminal offence committed in connection with a sporting event during participation in, travel to or departure from the sporting event.
(2) The shortest duration of the disqualification is one year, the longest duration is five years.
(3) * The duration of the disqualification begins when the decision on the merits becomes final. If disqualification from attending sporting events is imposed in addition to imprisonment, the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or evades the execution of the imprisonment shall not be included in its duration. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release shall be included in the duration of disqualification from attending sporting events.
5

## 9. §

(1) A perpetrator who is not a Hungarian citizen and whose presence in the country is undesirable shall be expelled from the territory of Hungary. The expelled person is obliged to leave the territory of the country and may not return during the duration of the expulsion.
(2) * A person to whom Hungary grants asylum may not be expelled.
(3) * Expulsion of a person with the right of free movement and residence and with the right of long-term residence in the territory of Hungary may only take place for committing a criminal offence punishable by imprisonment of five years or more.
(4) In respect of a person
a) who has been lawfully residing in the territory of Hungary for at least ten years, or
b) * who is lawfully residing in the territory of Hungary and whose right to respect for family life would be violated,
expulsion may only take place upon imposition of imprisonment of ten years or more, provided that the perpetrator's presence in the country would significantly endanger public security.
6

## 0. § (1) Expulsion is for a definite period or permanent

(2) The shortest duration of expulsion for a definite period is one year, the longest duration is ten years.
(2a) * In the case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B), obstructing construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C), and people smuggling (Section 35

## 3. §)

expulsion imposed in addition to imprisonment – with the exception of cases under Section 5

## 9. §

(2) and (4) – may not be dispensed with. In the case of expulsion for a definite period, its duration is twice the duration of the imposed imprisonment, but at least two years. The duration of expulsion may also be determined in years, months and days. When applying this paragraph, Section 5

## 0. § (2) there is no place for the application of paragraph (2)

(3) A person sentenced to imprisonment for a term of ten years or more may be expelled with final effect, and – having regard to the exceptional gravity of the offence, the nature of its commission, and the offender's connections – their stay in the country would significantly endanger public security. A person enjoying the right of free movement and residence may not be expelled with final effect.
(4) * The duration of expulsion begins on the day when the convicted person leaves the territory of Hungary – a convicted third-country national not enjoying the right of residence in another European Union Member State leaves the territory of the European Union – in accordance with the provisions set out in the decision on the execution of expulsion adopted under immigration legislation, or if this date is not known, the deadline designated for this purpose in the decision. If the court orders the expulsion of an accused person staying abroad or absent, the duration of expulsion begins on the day the final decision becomes final.
(5) The court may exempt the expelled person from final expulsion upon their request if ten years have elapsed since the expulsion and the expelled person is worthy of it.
6

## 1. §

(1) A person sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for committing an intentional criminal offence must be disqualified from exercising public affairs if they are unworthy to participate in their exercise.
(2) A person disqualified from public affairs
a) does not have the right to vote, may not participate in a referendum or popular initiative,
b) may not be an official,
c) may not be a member of the body or committee of a representative body, may not participate in their work,
d) may not be delegated to the assembly or body of an organisation established by an international treaty promulgated by law,
e) may not attain military rank,
f) may not receive a domestic decoration or permission to accept a foreign decoration,
g) may not be a defence counsel or legal representative in official proceedings,
h) may not hold office in a public body or public foundation, and
i) may not be a leading officer of a civil organisation as specified in the law on civil organisations.
(3) * A person disqualified from public affairs loses, upon the final decision becoming final, all membership, position, office, military rank, mandate and decoration the attainment of which is excluded by paragraph (2), as well as their honorary rank.
6

## 2. §

(1) Disqualification from public affairs is for a definite period, its shortest duration is one year, its longest duration is ten years.
(2) * The duration of disqualification from public affairs begins when the final decision becomes final. The duration of disqualification from public affairs does not include the time during which the convicted person serves the imprisonment or during which they evade the execution of the imprisonment. If conditional release is not revoked, the time spent on conditional release must be counted towards the duration of disqualification from public affairs.
6

## 3. § (1) Measures

a) reprimand,
b) probation,
c) reparation work,
d) probation supervision,
e) confiscation,
f) confiscation of assets,
g) making electronic data permanently inaccessible,
h) compulsory treatment,
i) measures under the law on criminal law measures applicable to legal persons.
(2) Reprimand, probation and reparation work may be applied independently, in place of punishment.
(3) Probation supervision may be applied alongside punishment or a measure. Probation supervision may not be ordered alongside expulsion.
(4) Confiscation, confiscation of assets and making electronic data permanently inaccessible may be applied independently and alongside punishment or a measure.
6

## 4. §

(1) A person whose act is no longer dangerous at the time of adjudication, or is dangerous to society to such a minor degree that the imposition of the minimum punishment applicable under this law or the application of another measure – not including confiscation, confiscation of assets and making electronic data permanently inaccessible – is unnecessary, must be given a reprimand.
(2) * By means of a reprimand, the court or the prosecution service expresses its disapproval of the unlawful act and calls upon the offender to refrain from committing criminal offences in the future.
6

## 5. §

(1) The court may postpone the imposition of punishment for a probationary period in the case of a misdemeanour and a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years if it can be reasonably assumed that the purpose of punishment can also be achieved by applying a measure.
(2) The following may not be placed on probation:
a) a recidivist,
b) a person who committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
c) a person who committed the intentional criminal offence after being sentenced to imprisonment to be executed, before the completion of execution, or
d) a person who committed the intentional criminal offence during the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment.
(3) The probationary period may range from one to three years; the duration must be determined in years, or in years and months.
(4) A person placed on probation may be placed under probation supervision. If the person placed on probation violates the rules of conduct of probation supervision, the probationary period may be extended once, by a maximum of one year.
6

## 6. §

(1) Probation must be revoked and punishment must be imposed if
a) the person placed on probation is convicted during the probationary period for a criminal offence committed before the probation,
b) the person placed on probation is convicted for a criminal offence committed during the probationary period, or
c) the person placed on probation seriously violates the rules of conduct of probation supervision.
(2) Apart from the cases specified in paragraph (1), upon expiry of the probationary period the offender's punishability ceases.
6

## 7. §

(1) The court may postpone the imposition of punishment for one year in the case of a misdemeanour and a felony punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, and prescribe the performance of reparation work, if it can be reasonably assumed that the purpose of punishment can thus also be achieved. Probation supervision may also be ordered alongside the prescription of reparation work.
(2) The performance of reparation work may not be prescribed for a person who
a) is a recidivist,
b) committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation,
c) committed the intentional criminal offence after being sentenced to imprisonment to be executed, before the completion of execution, or
d) committed the intentional criminal offence during the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment.
(3) * The offender – at their choice – may perform the reparation work at an institution maintained by the state or a local government, at a civil organisation with public benefit status, at an ecclesiastical legal person or for them.
(4) The duration of reparation work must be determined in hours; its minimum extent is twenty-four hours, its maximum extent is one hundred and fifty hours.
6

## 8. §

(1) If the offender duly proves the performance of reparation work within one year, their punishability ceases.
(2) * If the offender does not prove the performance of reparation work or seriously violates the rules of probation supervision, the court imposes punishment. If the offender is unable to prove the performance of reparation work for health reasons, the deadline for proving the performance of reparation work may be extended once by a maximum of one year.
6

## 9. § (1) * Probation supervision may be ordered

a) for the duration of conditional suspension by the prosecutor,
b) for the duration of conditional release,
c) for the probationary period of probation,
d) alongside the prescription of reparation work,
e) for the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment,
if regular monitoring of the offender is necessary for its successful completion.
(2) * The following are under probation supervision:
a) a person released on conditional release from life imprisonment,
b) a recidivist who has been released on conditional release or against whom the execution of imprisonment has been suspended,
c) an offender under § 3


## 8. §

(5) who has been released on conditional release under paragraph (5) of § 3

## 8. § (6) released on parole by application of paragraph,

d) for the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment, a person who has been sentenced to imprisonment for a violent offence against a person committed against their relative, or
e) for the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment, a person who has been sentenced to imprisonment for an offence against the freedom of sexual life and sexual morality committed against a person who has not reached the age of eighteen.
(3) * For the purposes of point d) of paragraph (2), relative shall also mean the parent of the offender's child, former spouse, former cohabitant, guardian, ward, custodian or person under custodianship.
7

## 0. § (1) * The duration of probation supervision is identical to

a) the duration of parole,
b) the probationary period of probation,
c) the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment,
d) the duration of conditional prosecutorial suspension,
but not more than five years, in the case of parole granted from life imprisonment not more than fifteen years.
(2) Probation supervision ordered in conjunction with the prescription of reparation work shall last until the person obliged to perform reparation work proves the completion of the reparation work, but for a maximum of one year.
(3) The 6

## 9. §

(1) in the case of paragraph, after half of the probation supervision has elapsed, but at least one year, the probation officer may propose the termination of probation supervision if its necessity no longer exists.
7

## 1. § (1) The probationer is obliged as a general rule of conduct to

a) comply with the rules of conduct prescribed by law and in the decision,
b) maintain regular contact with the probation officer, and
c) provide the probation officer with the information necessary for supervision.
(2) * The court, or in the case of conditional prosecutorial suspension the prosecution, may in its decision prescribe obligations and prohibitions as special rules of conduct to facilitate the purpose of probation supervision. The court or the prosecution may order that the probationer
a) shall not maintain contact with a specified person who participated in the commission of the offence,
b) * shall keep away from the victim of the offence, as well as from a relative or other person of the victim in consideration of the victim
(hereinafter together in the application of this §: affected person), from the affected person's residence or other property serving their habitual stay – including child welfare and child protection institutions –, workplace, institutions or other places regularly visited by the affected person, in particular educational, educational-training or health institutions visited for the purpose of medical treatment, buildings visited during the practice of religion,
c) * shall not visit public places and public events of a specified nature, as well as assemblies, specified public areas,
d) shall not consume alcoholic beverages in public places,
e) shall report at a specified place and at specified intervals to a specified authority or person,
f) shall contact the state employment service or register for public employment at the local government,
g) shall pursue specified studies,
h) – with their consent – shall undergo specified medical treatment or therapeutic procedure,
i) shall participate in group sessions organised by the probation officer or other sessions according to the programme of the community employment centre of the probation service.
(3) * The court or the prosecution may prescribe rules of conduct other than those listed in paragraph (2), with particular regard to the nature of the offence, the damage caused and the enhancement of the offender's chances of social integration.
(4) * The 6

## 9. §

(2) against an offender under points c) and d) of paragraph, the special rule of conduct under point b) of paragraph (2) must be ordered for the protection of the victim or a relative of the victim in consideration of the victim. The 6

## 9. §

(2) in the case under point c) of paragraph, the court shall order the use of a technical device tracking the movement of the obliged person to monitor compliance with this rule – except where the technical conditions for its application are not available.
(5) * The court may deviate from the application of paragraph (4) against an offender under point d) of paragraph (2) of § 6

## 9. §

having regard to the circumstances of the commission, the degree of danger to society inherent in the offender's person, the relationship between the offender and the victim of the offence or another person endangered in connection with the offence, or other sentencing circumstances.
(6) * The ordering of a restraining order under point b) of paragraph (2) does not preclude the offender – if entitled to contact – from maintaining contact with their minor child within the framework of supervised contact.
7

## 2. § (1) A thing shall be confiscated

a) which was used or intended as an instrument for the commission of the offence,
b) which came into being through the commission of an offence,
c) against which the offence was committed, or which was used for the purpose of transporting this thing after the completion of the offence,
d) the possession of which endangers public safety or is contrary to law.
(2) A press product in which the offence is realised shall be confiscated.
(3) In the cases of points a) and c) of paragraph (1) – provided that the owner did not know in advance of the commission – confiscation may not be ordered if the thing is not the property of the offender, except where the omission of confiscation is excluded by an international legal obligation.
(4) * Confiscation shall also be ordered if
a) the offender is not punishable due to childhood, morbid mental state or a ground for termination of punishability specified by law,
b) * the offender has been reprimanded,
c) it cannot be enforced during the period of special protection specified in the Act on the special protection of loaned cultural property.
(5) Confiscation of a thing to which asset confiscation extends may not be ordered.
(6) The ownership of a confiscated thing shall pass to the state in the absence of a contrary provision of law.
(7) There is no place for confiscation after the expiry of the time established for the limitation of punishability of the act, but at least five years.
7

## 3. §

The 7

## 2. §

(1) in the cases specified in points a) and c) of paragraph, confiscation may exceptionally be omitted if it would represent an unreasonable disadvantage to the offender or the owner that is not proportionate to the seriousness of the offence, except
a) if it is excluded by an international legal obligation,
b) if the offender committed the offence in a criminal organisation,
c) * in the case of drug trafficking, possession of drugs, facilitation of drug production, abuse of substances necessary for drug production, abuse of new psychoactive substances, abuse of performance-enhancing substances, pharmaceutical counterfeiting, counterfeiting of medical devices, abuse of poison, abuse of harmful consumer goods, environmental damage, damage to nature, animal cruelty, illegal fishing, illegal hunting, organisation of prohibited animal fights, violation of the waste management regime, abuse of ozone-depleting substances, abuse of radioactive materials, abuse in the operation of nuclear facilities, abuse of explosives or explosive devices, abuse of firearms or ammunition, abuse of weapons prohibited by international treaty, abuse of military technology products or services, abuse of dual-use products or violation of obligations related to the keeping of dangerous dogs.
7

## 4. § (1) Confiscation of assets shall be ordered on

a) assets derived from the commission of a criminal offence, which the perpetrator acquired during or in connection with the commission of the criminal offence,
b)–c) *
d) assets which replaced assets derived from the commission of a criminal offence, acquired during or in connection with the commission of the criminal offence,
e) assets which were provided or intended for the purpose of committing the criminal offence in order to ensure the conditions necessary for or facilitating it,
f) assets which were the subject of a pecuniary advantage given or promised.
(2) Confiscation of assets shall also be ordered on assets derived from the commission of a criminal offence, acquired during or in connection with the commission of the criminal offence, by which another person was enriched. If a business organisation was enriched by such assets, confiscation of assets shall be ordered against the business organisation.
(3) * If the perpetrator or the person enriched pursuant to paragraph (2) has died, or the business organisation has been transformed, confiscation of assets shall be ordered against the legal successor on the assets pursuant to paragraph (1) or Section 74/A. in respect of which succession occurred.
(4) *
(5) Confiscation of assets may not be ordered
a) on assets which serve as security for a civil law claim asserted during criminal proceedings,
b) on assets which were acquired in good faith for consideration,
c) * on assets pursuant to paragraph (1) points a) and d) or paragraph (2), if in relation to them, on the basis of the same facts as the criminal offence which is the subject of the criminal proceedings, an administrative authority or state tax and customs authority has established a payment obligation in a final decision or a court has established it in a final judgment in administrative proceedings, up to the amount of the payment obligation.
74/A. § * (1) Until proven otherwise, assets shall be considered subject to confiscation, and confiscation of assets shall be ordered on assets which the perpetrator acquired during the time of
a) his participation in a criminal organisation,
b) the commission of placing narcotic drugs on the market or trading in them,
c) the commission of people smuggling committed in a business-like manner or in a criminal association.
(2) Until proven otherwise, assets shall also be considered subject to confiscation, and confiscation of assets shall be ordered on assets which

## 4. §),

o) budget fraud committed in a criminal organisation causing particularly large or particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage [39

## 6. § (4)–(5)],

p) money laundering (39

## 9. §),

q) unlawful acquisition of data committed in a criminal association or in a business-like manner [42

## 4. §

(1)] the perpetrator acquired in the five years preceding the initiation of criminal proceedings, if the assets or the perpetrator's lifestyle is particularly disproportionate to his demonstrable income circumstances and personal circumstances.
(3) Confiscation of assets may not be ordered in the cases of paragraphs (1) and (2) if the perpetrator proves that the assets do not derive from a criminal offence.
7

## 5. § (1) Confiscation of assets shall be ordered expressed as a sum of money, *

a) * if the assets subject to confiscation can no longer be found,
b) * if the assets subject to confiscation cannot be separated from other assets, or their separation would cause disproportionate difficulty,
c) in the case specified in Section 7

## 4. § (5) point b)

(2) * Confiscation of assets shall also be ordered if
a) the perpetrator is not punishable due to childhood, morbid mental state or a ground for terminating punishability specified by law,
b) * the perpetrator was given a reprimand,
c) it cannot be executed during the period of special protection specified in the law on the special protection of loaned cultural property.
(3) Confiscated assets shall pass to the state unless otherwise provided by law.
7

## 6. §

For the purposes of this subsection, assets shall also be understood to mean the benefit thereof, a right or claim of pecuniary value, and any advantage having a value expressible in money.
7

## 7. §

(1) Data published on an electronic communications network must be made permanently inaccessible if
a) making it accessible or publishing it constitutes a criminal offence,
b) it was used as an instrument for committing a criminal offence, or
c) it was created through the commission of a criminal offence.
(2) The permanent inaccessibility of electronic data must also be ordered if the perpetrator is not punishable due to childhood, pathological mental state, or a reason terminating punishability as defined by law, or if the perpetrator was admonished.
7

## 8. §

(1) Compulsory medical treatment must be ordered for the perpetrator of a punishable act involving violence against a person or causing public danger if they are not punishable due to a pathological state of their mental functioning, and there is reason to fear that they will commit a similar act, provided that if they were punishable, a penalty more severe than one year's imprisonment would have to be imposed.
(2) Compulsory medical treatment must be terminated if its necessity no longer exists.
7

## 9. §

The purpose of punishment is to prevent both the perpetrator and others from committing criminal offences in the interest of protecting society.
8

## 0. §

(1) Punishment must be imposed within the limits defined in this Act, keeping its purpose in mind, so that it corresponds to the objective gravity of the criminal offence, the degree of culpability, the dangerousness of the perpetrator to society, and other mitigating and aggravating circumstances.
(2) When imposing imprisonment for a definite period, the middle measure of the sentencing range is decisive. The middle measure is half of the sum of the lower and upper limits of the sentencing range.
(3) If this Act prescribes an increase in the sentencing ranges defined in the Special Part when imposing punishment, the calculation specified in paragraph (2) must be performed with regard to the increased sentencing ranges.
(4) If the court imposes imprisonment, it determines the measure of punishment disregarding the possibility of suspension of execution or conditional release.
8

## 1. § (1) In case of concurrence of offences, one punishment must be imposed

(2) The cumulative punishment must be imposed on the basis of the most severe among the types of punishment or sentencing ranges established for the criminal offences in concurrence.
(3) If at least two of the criminal offences in concurrence are punishable by imprisonment for a definite period, the upper limit of the sentencing range is increased by half of the highest sentencing range, but may not reach the combined duration of the upper limits of the sentencing ranges established for the individual criminal offences.
(4) * If the conditions therefor exist in relation to the given criminal offence, the sentencing range for the criminal offence in concurrence must be established on the basis of the provisions concerning special, multiple and violent multiple recidivists, as well as commission in a criminal organisation, prior to the application of paragraphs (2) and (3).
(5) Accessory punishment in case of cumulative punishment may not exceed the highest measure or duration defined by law.
8

## 2. §

(1) A punishment more lenient than the sentencing range may be imposed if its minimum measure would be too severe having regard to the principles of imposing punishment.
(2) On the basis of paragraph (1), if the lower limit of the sentencing range is
a) ten years' imprisonment, at least five years',
b) five years' imprisonment, at least two years',
c) two years' imprisonment, at least one year's,
d) one year's imprisonment, imprisonment of shorter duration
may be imposed instead.
(3) In the case of paragraph (2) point d), instead of imprisonment, detention, community service work or fine, or these punishments may also be imposed alongside each other.
(4) In case of attempt or aiding and abetting, if the punishment imposable on the basis of paragraph (2) would also be too severe, the punishment may be imposed on the basis of the next point of paragraph (2).
(5) If this Act permits unlimited mitigation, the minimum measure of any type of punishment may also be imposed.
8

## 3. §

(1) * In proceedings under Chapter XCIX of Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, when imposing punishment in case of approval of an agreement (hereinafter: agreement), the lower limit of the more lenient sentencing ranges defined in 8

## 2. §

paragraph (2) must be taken as a basis.
(2) * If the agreement is approved in the case of a defendant who, contributing to the proof of the case or another criminal case, cooperated with the prosecutor or the investigating authority to a significant extent, the punishment may also be imposed on the basis of the next point of paragraph (2) of 8

## 2. §

(3) *
8

## 4. § *

8

## 5. §

(1) The execution of imprisonment not exceeding two years may be suspended for a probationary period if – particularly having regard to the personal circumstances of the perpetrator – it can reasonably be assumed that the purpose of the punishment can be achieved without its execution.
(1a) * In case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A of the Criminal Code), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B of the Criminal Code), and obstructing construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C of the Criminal Code), the execution of imprisonment not exceeding five years may be suspended for a probationary period.
(2) The duration of the probationary period – unless this Act provides otherwise – may range from one year to five years, but may not be shorter than the imposed imprisonment. The probationary period must be determined in years, or in years and months.
(2a) * In case of illegal crossing of the border barrier (Section 352/A of the Criminal Code), damaging the border barrier (Section 352/B of the Criminal Code), and obstructing construction work related to the border barrier (Section 352/C of the Criminal Code), the duration of the probationary period may range from two years to ten years, but may not be shorter than the duration of expulsion imposed for these criminal offences.
8

## 6. §

(1) The execution of imprisonment may not be suspended against a person who
a) is a multiple recidivist,
b) committed the criminal offence in a criminal organisation, or
c) committed the intentional criminal offence before the completion of the execution of imprisonment or during the probationary period of its suspension.
(2) If imprisonment is being executed on the perpetrator due to which the execution of the suspended imprisonment cannot be ordered, then the probationary period is extended by the duration of the imprisonment.
(3) The provision of paragraph (2) must also be applied in case of imprisonment replacing community service work and fine.
(4) In the cases of paragraphs (2) and (3), the duration of the probationary period may exceed five years.
(5) If the perpetrator is sentenced to suspended imprisonment on multiple occasions and the probationary periods of the imprisonments have not yet elapsed, all probationary periods run concurrently.
(6) Simultaneously with the suspension of the execution of imprisonment, the perpetrator may be placed under probation supervision. If the perpetrator is a recidivist, they are under probation supervision.
8

## 7. § The suspended imprisonment must be executed if

a) during the probationary period it is established that the execution of imprisonment was suspended despite the exclusionary ground set out in paragraph (1) of 8

## 6. §,

b) the perpetrator is sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for a criminal offence committed during the probationary period, or
c) the perpetrator seriously violates the rules of conduct of probation supervision,
d) * the perpetrator expelled on the basis of paragraph (2a) of 6

## 0. §

illegally returns to the territory of Hungary during the probationary period of the suspended imprisonment.
8

## 8. §

In case of suspension of the execution of punishment by pardon, the provision on the execution of suspended punishment must be applied accordingly to the ordering of execution.
8

## 9. §

(1) * Against a special and multiple recidivist, the upper limit of the sentencing range for the new criminal offence is increased by half in case of imprisonment, but may not exceed twenty-five years.
(2) Against a special and multiple recidivist, the punishment is 8

## 2. §

may be mitigated only in cases deserving special consideration on the basis of paragraph (1).
(3) The more severe legal consequences defined in paragraph (1) may not be applied if the Special Part of this Act provides for commission as a special recidivist as a more severely qualified case of the offence.
9

## 0. §

(1) With regard to a violent multiple recidivist, 3

## 3. § paragraph (4) may not be applied

(2) * With regard to a violent multiple recidivist, the upper limit of the sentencing range for the offence establishing the classification as a violent multiple recidivist shall be doubled in the case of imprisonment. If the upper limit of the sentencing range thus increased would exceed twenty years, or if the offence is punishable by life imprisonment under the law, life imprisonment shall be imposed on the offender.
(3) With regard to a violent multiple recidivist, the punishment
a) may not be mitigated on the basis of 8

## 2. § paragraph (1),

b) – if the General Part of this Act permits it – may be mitigated without limitation.
9

## 1. §

(1) * With regard to a person who committed an intentional offence in a criminal organisation, the upper limit of the sentencing range for the offence shall be doubled, but may not exceed twenty-five years.
(2) With regard to a person who committed an offence in a criminal organisation, expulsion may also be imposed.
(3) Where commission of an offence in a criminal organisation is established, the legal consequences provided for in this Act for commission of an offence in complicity may not be applied.
9

## 2. §

(1) * The entire period of pre-trial detention and of such criminal supervision during which the court ordered the accused not to leave a dwelling, other premises, institution, or the fenced area belonging thereto without permission shall be credited towards the imposed imprisonment, detention, community service work and fine.
(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of pre-trial detention corresponds to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, one day-unit of fine, or four hours of community service work, respectively.
(3) * For the purposes of crediting
a) one day of imprisonment corresponds to five days in the case of fortress grade, four days in the case of prison grade, three days in the case of jail grade,
b) one day of detention corresponds to two days,
c) four hours of community service work corresponds to one day,
d) one day-unit of fine corresponds to one day
of the period spent under criminal supervision as defined in paragraph (1).
(4) * The period remaining after crediting pursuant to paragraphs (2) and (3) shall be credited as one day of imprisonment.
92/A. § * (1) * The entire duration of detention and community service work imposed and executed in misdemeanour proceedings, as well as on-the-spot fines and pecuniary fines, shall be credited – following a prosecutor's notice, a review or reopening procedure conducted pursuant to the Act on Misdemeanours, in respect of the act affected by the notice, review or reopening – towards the imprisonment, detention, community service work, fine and reformatory education imposed in criminal proceedings.
(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of misdemeanour detention corresponds to one day of imprisonment or detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, and one day of reformatory education, respectively.
(3) * An on-the-spot fine and a misdemeanour pecuniary fine correspond, per five thousand forints, to one day of imprisonment or detention, four hours of community service work, the same value of fine, or one day of reformatory education, respectively.
(4) Community service work imposed in misdemeanour proceedings corresponds to the same amount of community service work, and furthermore, community service work imposed in misdemeanour proceedings corresponds, per six hours, to one day of imprisonment or detention, one day-unit of fine, and one day of reformatory education, respectively.
92/B. § * (1) * If the judgment rendered as a result of reopening, review, remedy lodged in the interest of legality, or repeated proceedings modifies the decision rendered in the underlying case, the punishment or measure imposed and executed in the underlying case shall be credited. If the type of punishment or measure differs, the provisions of paragraph (2) shall apply to the crediting.
(2) For the purposes of crediting
a) one day of imprisonment corresponds to one day of detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
b) one day of detention corresponds to one day of imprisonment, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
c) four hours of community service work correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, one day-unit of fine, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
d) one day-unit of fine corresponds to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, four hours of community service work, six hours of reparation work or one day of reformatory education,
e) one day of reformatory education corresponds to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine or six hours of reparation work,
f) six hours of reparation work correspond to one day of imprisonment, one day of detention, four hours of community service work, one day-unit of fine or one day of reformatory education,
respectively.
9

## 3. §

* (1) * If the offender is sentenced to multiple terms of imprisonment for a definite period, the sentences imposed with final effect shall be combined into a cumulative sentence – as provided by law – if the offender committed all the offences before the announcement of the earliest first-instance decision on the merits.
(2) Only such terms of imprisonment to be executed may be combined into a cumulative sentence which have not yet been executed at the time of combining into a cumulative sentence, or which are being executed continuously.
(3) If a suspended term of imprisonment is to be executed subsequently, it shall be considered as imprisonment to be executed for the purposes of combining into a cumulative sentence.
(4) The following may not be combined into a cumulative sentence:
a) a punishment previously combined into a cumulative sentence,
b) imprisonment substituted for a fine or community service work.
9

## 4. §

* The term of the cumulative sentence shall be determined as if a concurrent sentence were being imposed. However, the term of the cumulative sentence must reach the term calculated as the sum of the most severe punishment and one-third of the shorter punishment or punishments, but may not exceed the aggregate term of the punishments.
9

## 5. §

* (1) When combining terms of imprisonment to be executed in different grades into a cumulative sentence, the most stringent grade shall be determined as the grade of execution. If the measure of the cumulative sentence is three years or more, or in the case of a multiple recidivist two years or more, the grade of execution of the cumulative sentence shall be determined taking this into account.
(2) If the grade of execution to be determined by applying paragraph (1) would result in an unjust disadvantage for the convicted person, a grade one level more lenient may be determined.
