(3) * If the possibility of conditional release is not excluded, its earliest date shall be determined on the basis of the most stringent provision. The convicted person may not be conditionally released from the cumulative sentence if the possibility of conditional release is excluded in respect of any of the terms of imprisonment.
(4) * If the term of imprisonment in connection with which the convicted person was excluded from the possibility of conditional release due to his or her own fault is subsequently combined into a cumulative sentence, the convicted person may not be conditionally released from the cumulative sentence either.
9

## 6. §

* (1) If disqualification from an occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion, disqualification from attending sporting events or expulsion from the country was imposed alongside imprisonment, and the imprisonment sentences were combined into a cumulative sentence, then among several disqualifications from an occupation, disqualifications from driving, expulsions, disqualifications from attending sporting events or expulsions from the country of the same content, the one that is more disadvantageous to the convicted person shall be executed.
(2) The accessory punishment of disqualification from public affairs cannot be combined into a cumulative sentence. Among several accessory punishments of disqualification from public affairs, the one with the longest duration shall be executed.
9

## 7. §

(1) Adverse legal consequences attached to the establishment of criminal liability, the imposition of punishment or the application of a measure may be established by law.
(2) * Data relating to the establishment of criminal liability, the imposed punishment and the applied measure shall be contained in an authentic official register until the time specified by law, after which no adverse legal consequence may be established against the convicted person on account of the conviction. Recidivism and the adverse legal consequences attached to it as defined in this Act shall be established on the basis of data obtained from the criminal records at the time closest to the commission of the new criminal offence, in accordance with Act XC of 2017 on Criminal Procedure, even if these data are subsequently deleted.
9

## 9. §

a

## 8. §

(1) As a result of rehabilitation – in the absence of a contrary provision of law – the convicted person shall be relieved of the adverse legal consequences attached to a criminal record.
(2) A rehabilitated person shall be considered to have a clean criminal record and – in the absence of a contrary provision of law – is not required to account for any conviction in respect of which he or she has been rehabilitated.
(3) In the event of the commission of a new criminal offence, rehabilitation does not extend to those criminal law consequences which this Act attaches to the previous conviction.
9

## 9. § The convicted person may be granted rehabilitation

a) by operation of this Act,
b) on the basis of a court decision, or
c) by way of pardon.
10

## 0. § (1) Rehabilitation occurs by operation of this Act

a) * in the case of detention, fine and community service, on the date on which the decision on the merits becomes final,
b) * in the case of disqualification from an occupation, disqualification from driving, expulsion and disqualification from attending sporting events, on the date on which the decision on the merits becomes final,
c) in the case of expulsion from the country, on the date of completion of the execution of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability,
d) in the case of suspended imprisonment, on the date of expiry of the probationary period,
e) in the case of imprisonment imposed for a negligent misdemeanour, on the date of completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability,
f) in the case of imprisonment not exceeding one year imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of three years following the completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability,
g) in the case of imprisonment exceeding one year but not longer than five years imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of five years following the completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability,
h) in the case of imprisonment exceeding five years but not longer than ten years imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of eight years following the completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability,
i) in the case of imprisonment for a fixed term exceeding ten years imposed for an intentional criminal offence, upon the expiry of ten years following the completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability.
(2) In the case of paragraph (1) point b), rehabilitation does not extend to
a) in the case of disqualification from an occupation, the right to practise the occupation,
b) in the case of disqualification from driving, the right to practise driving,
c) in the case of expulsion, the right to stay in the locality specified in the judgment or in a specified part of the country,
d) in the case of disqualification from attending sporting events, the convicted person's right to attend sporting events.
(3) * In the case of paragraph (1) point d), rehabilitation does not occur if the execution of the imprisonment is ordered. In such case, the rehabilitation rules applicable to enforceable imprisonment shall apply to rehabilitation.
10

## 1. §

* (1) In the case of enforceable imprisonment, the court may grant the convicted person rehabilitation retrospectively upon request if he or she is worthy of it and half of the time specified in paragraph (1) points f) to i) of Section 10

## 0. § has already elapsed since the completion of the imprisonment or the termination of its enforceability

(2) In the case of suspended imprisonment, the court may grant the convicted person rehabilitation retrospectively upon request if he or she is worthy of it and half of the probationary period, but at least one year, has already elapsed.
(3) * In assessing worthiness, account must be taken of the convicted person's way of life since the completion of the sentence, or in the case of suspended imprisonment since the decision on the merits became final, and also whether – if he or she had the opportunity to do so – he or she has made good the harm caused by the criminal offence.
(4) In the case of suspended imprisonment, retrospective rehabilitation loses its effect if the execution of the suspended imprisonment is ordered.
10

## 2. §

(1) * In the case of suspension of the execution of imprisonment, the court may grant the convicted person advance rehabilitation in the decision on the merits if he or she is worthy of it.
(2) Advance rehabilitation loses its effect if the execution of the suspended imprisonment is ordered.
10

## 3. §

In the case of the application of an accessory punishment, the convicted person shall not be relieved of, or may not be relieved of, the adverse legal consequences attached to the conviction until the execution of the accessory punishment has been completed or its enforceability has ceased.
10

## 4. §

(1) The person exercising the power of pardon may grant the convicted person rehabilitation by way of pardon even if there would otherwise be no grounds for this under this Act.
(2) A person granted pardon rehabilitation shall be considered to have a clean criminal record for the purposes of adverse legal consequences outside criminal law.
10

## 5. §

(1) A juvenile is a person who has reached the age of twelve but not eighteen at the time of committing the criminal offence.
(2) The provisions of this Act shall apply to juveniles with the exceptions set out in this Chapter.
10

## 6. §

(1) The purpose of a punishment imposed on or a measure applied to a juvenile is primarily to ensure that the juvenile develops in the right direction and becomes a useful member of society; accordingly, when choosing a measure or punishment, the upbringing and protection of the juvenile must be kept in mind.
(2) A punishment shall be imposed on a juvenile if the application of a measure is not effective. Only a measure may be applied to a person who has not reached the age of fourteen at the time of committing the criminal offence.
(3) A measure involving deprivation of liberty may be applied to a juvenile or a punishment involving deprivation of liberty may be imposed only if the purpose of the measure or punishment cannot be achieved by other means.
10

## 7. §

If the offender is a juvenile, active repentance may also take place in the case of a misdemeanour or a felony punishable by no more than five years' imprisonment as defined in paragraph (1) of Section 2

## 9. §

10

## 8. §

(1) Reformatory education may also be applied as a measure against a juvenile.
(2) No imprisonment, detention or community service may be imposed alongside reformatory education.
10

## 9. §

(1) The minimum duration of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile is one month for any criminal offence.
(2) The maximum duration of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile who had not attained the age of sixteen at the time of committing the criminal offence is
a) ten years in the case of committing a criminal offence punishable by life imprisonment,
b) five years in the case of committing a criminal offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years.
(3) The maximum duration of imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile who had attained the age of sixteen at the time of committing the criminal offence is
a) fifteen years in the case of committing a criminal offence punishable by life imprisonment,
b) ten years in the case of committing a criminal offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding ten years,
c) five years in the case of committing a criminal offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years.
(4) For the purposes of calculating the limitation period for criminal liability and the provisions on recidivists, the time periods specified in paragraphs (2)–(3) shall be decisive.
(5) * In the case of a juvenile, the possibility of parole under 3

## 8. §

(4) point e) and paragraph (5) may be excluded if the juvenile had attained the age of sixteen at the time of committing the criminal offence and is sentenced to imprisonment of ten years or more.
11

## 0. § (1) The enforcement level of imprisonment is juvenile prison if

a) the juvenile is sentenced to imprisonment of two years or longer for a felony,
b) the juvenile sentenced to imprisonment of one year or longer is a recidivist, or
c) the juvenile sentenced to imprisonment of one year or longer was sentenced to reformatory education for an intentional criminal offence within three years preceding the commission of the intentional criminal offence.
(2) In cases other than those specified in paragraph (1), the enforcement level of imprisonment is juvenile jail.
11

## 1. §

The minimum duration of detention that may be imposed on a juvenile is three days, the maximum duration is thirty days.
11

## 2. §

* Community service may be imposed on a juvenile if the juvenile had attained the age of sixteen at the time the decision on the merits is made.
11

## 3. §

* (1) A fine may be imposed on a juvenile if the juvenile has independent earnings, income or adequate assets.
(2) * In the case of a juvenile, the minimum amount of the fine is fifteen, the maximum amount is two hundred and fifty day-fines, the amount of one day-fine shall be determined at a minimum of six hundred and fifty, at a maximum of sixty-five thousand forints.
(3) In the case of a juvenile, if the fine is unenforceable, it shall be converted into
a) community service if 11

## 2. § permits it, or

b) imprisonment.
(4) The community service replacing the fine shall be determined so that two hours of community service replace one day-fine. Otherwise, the provisions of 4

## 7. §

shall apply to community service replacing the fine, with the proviso that its duration may differ from those specified in 4

## 7. § (1)

(5) If the juvenile does not voluntarily fulfil the work obligation, the community service or the remaining part thereof shall be converted into imprisonment. Otherwise, the provisions of 4

## 8. § shall apply

113/A. § * In the case of a juvenile, the application of 5

## 2. § (3) may be waived in cases deserving special consideration

11

## 4. § Expulsion may be imposed on a juvenile if

a) the juvenile has been sentenced to imprisonment of ten years or more,
b) the juvenile's stay in the country would significantly endanger public security, and
c) the juvenile's right to respect for family life is not violated.
11

## 5. §

A juvenile may be disqualified from public affairs only if sentenced to imprisonment exceeding one year.
11

## 6. §

(1) Probation may be applied to a juvenile for any criminal offence.
(2) The probationary period may range from one to two years.
(3) In the cases specified in 6

## 6. §

(1), the court shall order reformatory education or impose a punishment.
11

## 7. §

* Reparation work may be prescribed for a juvenile if the juvenile had attained the age of sixteen at the time the decision on the merits is made.
11

## 8. §

A juvenile with an appropriate family environment may not be banned from the settlement where the juvenile's family lives.
11

## 9. § (1) * A juvenile

a) during the period of parole,
b) during the probationary period of probation,
c) during the probationary period of suspension of imprisonment,
d) during the period of temporary release from the reformatory,
e) during the period of conditional prosecutorial suspension
is under probation supervision.
(2) Probation supervision shall also be ordered for a juvenile in addition to prescribing reparation work.
(3) * In the case of a juvenile, 7

## 1. § (4) shall not apply

12

## 0. §

(1) * The court shall order reformatory education if institutional placement of the juvenile is necessary for the juvenile's successful upbringing. Reformatory education may not be ordered against a person who had attained the age of twenty at the time the decision on the merits is made.
(2) The duration of reformatory education may range from one to four years.
12

## 1. §

(1) When ordering reformatory education, the court shall establish that the juvenile may be temporarily released from the institution after serving half of the reformatory education if
a) the juvenile has spent at least one year in the institution, and
b) it can be reasonably assumed that the purpose of the measure can be achieved without further reformatory education.
(2) The duration of temporary release is equal to the remaining part of the reformatory education, but at least one year.
(3) The court shall terminate the temporary release if the juvenile is sentenced to imprisonment during the temporary release – except for the case specified in 12

## 2. §

– or reformatory education is ordered against the juvenile. If the court imposes another punishment on the juvenile or applies another measure, the court may terminate the temporary release.
(4) In the event of termination of temporary release, the time spent on temporary release may not be credited towards the reformatory education.
12

## 2. §

If the juvenile is sentenced to enforceable imprisonment for a criminal offence committed after being sentenced to reformatory education, during the reformatory education or during the temporary release, the imprisonment sentence shall be enforced. In this case, the remaining part of the reformatory education shall be converted into imprisonment so that two days of reformatory education are replaced by one day of imprisonment.
12

## 3. § (1) In the case of a juvenile, the cumulative sentence and the aggregate sentence may not exceed

a) twenty years in the case of 10

## 9. § (3) point a),

b) fifteen years in the case of 10

## 9. § (2) point a) and (3) point b),

c) seven years and six months in the case of 10

## 9. §

(2) point b) and (3) point c)
of imprisonment.
(2) * The imprisonment that may be imposed on a juvenile may not exceed the amounts specified in (1) points a) and b) even when applying 9

## 0. §

(2).
(3) In the case of a combination of reformatory education and imprisonment, the imprisonment shall be enforced as an aggregate sentence. The court may extend its duration by a maximum of one year if this is necessary to achieve the purpose specified in 10

## 6. §

The duration of the extension, however, may not reach the remaining part of the reformatory education.
12

## 4. §

(1) If the court has ordered multiple correctional institution educations against the juvenile which have not yet been executed at the time of ordering the unified measure, or which are being executed continuously, the court shall order correctional institution education as a unified measure against the juvenile.
(2) The duration of the correctional institution education ordered as a unified measure shall be determined so that it is not shorter than the longest-duration correctional institution education, but does not exceed the combined duration of the ordered correctional institution educations, or four years.
124/A. § * (1) * If the approval of the agreement takes place in the case of a juvenile who had not reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offence, and who cooperated significantly with the prosecutor or the investigating authority by contributing to the proof of the case or other criminal case, the imposable term of imprisonment
a) in the case of committing an offence punishable by life imprisonment, may not exceed eight years,
b) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years, may not exceed three years,
c) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding three years, may not exceed two years,
d) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, may not exceed six months.
(2) * If the approval of the agreement takes place in the case of a juvenile who had reached the age of sixteen at the time of committing the offence, and who cooperated significantly with the prosecutor or the investigating authority by contributing to the proof of the case or other criminal case, the imposable term of imprisonment
a) in the case of committing an offence punishable by life imprisonment, may not exceed ten years,
b) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding ten years, may not exceed eight years,
c) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding five years, may not exceed three years,
d) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment exceeding three years, may not exceed two years,
e) in the case of committing an offence punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, may not exceed six months.
(3) In imposing the punishment
a) in the case of concurrence of offences,
b) against a special, multiple or violent multiple recidivist, or
c) in the case of committing an offence in a criminal organisation
the penalty range according to subsections (1) and (2) shall be taken as a basis.
12

## 5. §

* (1) The full period of pre-trial detention and of such criminal supervision during which the court prescribed for the accused that he or she may not leave a dwelling, other premises, institution, or the fenced area belonging thereto without permission, shall be credited towards the ordered correctional institution education.
(2) For the purposes of crediting, one day of correctional institution education corresponds to
a) one day of pre-trial detention, and
b) three days of criminal supervision as defined in subsection (1).
(3) The duration remaining after crediting shall be credited as one day of correctional institution education.
12

## 6. § (1) The convicted juvenile is released by operation of law

a) * on the day the decision on the merits becomes final, if the execution of the imposed imprisonment is suspended,
b) on the day of completion of the sentence or cessation of its enforceability, if sentenced to imprisonment not exceeding one year for an intentional offence,
c) upon the expiry of three years from the day of completion of the sentence or cessation of its enforceability, if sentenced to enforceable imprisonment exceeding one year but not longer than five years for an intentional offence.
(2) After completion of imprisonment exceeding one year imposed for an intentional offence, the court may grant release to the juvenile upon request, if he or she is worthy thereof.
12

## 7. §

(1) * For the purposes of this Act, a soldier is a member of the professional staff of the armed forces, the police, the Parliamentary Guard, the penitentiary organisation, the professional disaster management body, and the civilian national security services.
(2) The provisions of this Act shall apply to soldiers with the exceptions set out in this Chapter.
(3) A military offence as a principal may only be committed by a soldier.
12

## 8. §

(1) A soldier is punishable under Chapter XLV if he or she commits the military offence
a) against a soldier of an allied armed force, or
b) against a soldier of another state in a foreign theatre of operations during humanitarian activity, peacekeeping or humanitarian operation, or during other foreign service
in the course of joint performance of service duties.
(2) For the purposes of point a) of subsection (1), a soldier of an allied armed force includes a member of the police or civil defence service of the state of the allied armed force; for the purposes of point b) of subsection (1), a soldier of another state includes a member of the police or civil defence service of another state.
12

## 9. §

A soldier is also punishable under Chapter XLV if he or she commits the military offence
a) during service performed in the allied armed forces,
b) in a foreign theatre of operations during humanitarian activity, peacekeeping or humanitarian operation.
13

## 0. §

(1) A soldier is not punishable for an act performed pursuant to an order, except if he or she knew that by executing the order he or she would commit an offence.
(2) For an offence committed pursuant to an order, the person giving the order is also liable as a principal if the soldier knew that by executing the order he or she would commit an offence; otherwise the person giving the order is liable as an indirect principal.
13

## 1. §

A 6

## 6. §

Except for the cases specified in subsection (1), the perpetrator is not punishable for a military misdemeanour if one year has elapsed since the termination of his or her service relationship.
13

## 2. §

(1) If the convict can be retained in service, the court shall order the execution of imprisonment not exceeding one year, or detention, imposed for a misdemeanour in a military detention facility, except if the convict is a recidivist.
(2) If the service relationship of the convict has been terminated, the level of execution of the sentence or its remaining part is prison.
13

## 3. §

For the execution of imprisonment imposed as an aggregate sentence, 13

## 2. § is applicable

13

## 4. §

Community service may not be imposed on a soldier during the existence of his or her service relationship.
13

## 5. § (1) Against a soldier

a) demotion, or
b) termination of service relationship
may be imposed in addition to another punishment, or, if the upper limit of the penalty range of the offence is not more severe than three years' imprisonment, also independently.
(2) There is no place for the application of a military punishment if the soldier is disqualified from public affairs.
(3) * The punishment of termination of service relationship may not be applied against a person liable for military service.
13

## 6. § (1) Against a soldier

a) reduction in rank, or
b) extension of waiting period
may be imposed in addition to a punishment.
(2) There is no place for the application of a military accessory punishment in addition to a military punishment, or if the soldier is disqualified from public affairs.
13

## 7. §

(1) * By demotion, the soldier loses his or her rank and honorary rank.
(2) Demotion shall be applied if the perpetrator has become unworthy of the rank.
13

## 8. §

There is place for termination of service relationship if the perpetrator has become unworthy of service.
13

## 9. §

(1) In the case of reduction in rank, the soldier is placed in a rank one level lower than the one he or she holds at the time of adjudication of the offence.
(2) Reduction in rank shall be applied if the offence involves harm to the prestige of the rank, but demotion is not necessary.
(3) Simultaneously with reduction in rank, the time to be spent in the lower rank shall be determined for a period ranging from one year to two years.
14

## 0. §

(1) * In the case of extension of the waiting period, the waiting period prescribed for the promotion of the soldier to the next rank shall be extended. The extension shall be determined in years and its duration may not exceed half of the waiting period prescribed for the rank.
(1a) * In the case of extension of the waiting period, for professional members of the police, the Parliamentary Guard, the prison service, the professional disaster management body and the civilian national security services, the waiting period prescribed for advancement to the next pay grade shall be extended. The extension shall be determined in years and its duration may not exceed half of the waiting period assigned to the pay grade.
(2) * The waiting period shall be extended if the soldier must earn the promotion or the advancement by completing a longer waiting period.
(3) * In the case of soldiers, the waiting period specified in paragraphs (1) and (2) shall mean the mandatory waiting period.
14

## 1. §

* (1) * Discharge occurs by operation of law in the case of demotion and termination of service relationship on the date when the decision on the merits becomes final.
(2) * The court may grant the convicted person advance discharge from the disadvantages attached to criminal record in the decision on the merits if it orders the imprisonment to be executed in a military detention facility. This discharge takes effect on the date of completion of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability.
(3) The application of a military accessory punishment does not prevent the discharge of the convicted person.
14

## 2. §

(1) Whoever, with the intent to destroy, in whole or in part, a national, ethnic, racial or religious group,
a) kills members of the group,
b) causes serious bodily or mental harm to members of the group,
c) forces the group into living conditions that threaten it or its individual members with destruction,
d) takes measures aimed at preventing births within the group,
e) abducts children belonging to the group to another group,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.
(2) Whoever commits preparation for genocide shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.
14

## 3. § (1) Whoever, as part of a widespread or systematic attack against a civilian population,

a) commits homicide,
b) forces the population or a part thereof into living conditions that threaten it or its individual members with destruction,
c) displaces the population or a part thereof from its lawful place of residence,
d) * commits human trafficking and forced labour,
e) deprives another of personal liberty or unlawfully maintains their detention,
f) forces another to sexual violence or to tolerate it, to prostitution, to carry a foetus to term or to abortion,
g) causes serious bodily or mental harm to another,
h) deprives a group defined on the basis of political, national, ethnic, cultural, religious, gender or other characteristics, or a member thereof, of fundamental rights on account of belonging to the group,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.
(2) Whoever commits preparation for a crime against humanity shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.
(3) For the purposes of this Section, a widespread or systematic attack against a civilian population means any conduct involving the serial commission of the acts described in paragraph (1) against a civilian population, in furtherance of or to promote a policy of a state or organisation.
14

## 4. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of establishing and maintaining the domination of one racial group of persons over another racial group of persons, or the systematic oppression of the other racial group,
a) kills members of a racial group or groups,
b) forces a racial group or groups into living conditions aimed at bringing about the total or partial physical destruction of the group or groups,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.
(2) Whoever commits other crimes of apartheid shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment if the other crime of apartheid leads to serious consequences.
(4) Whoever commits preparation for apartheid,
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), from five to ten years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), from two to eight years
shall be punishable by imprisonment.
(5) For the purposes of paragraphs (2)–(3), other crimes of apartheid means the crimes of apartheid defined in points a)/(ii), a)/(iii), c), d), e) and f) of Article II of the International Convention on the Suppression and Punishment of the Crime of Apartheid, adopted by the General Assembly of the United Nations in New York on 30 November 1973, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 27 of 1976.
14

## 5. § The following shall be held liable in the same manner as the perpetrator of a crime defined in this Chapter:

a) a military superior or a person actually acting as a military superior (hereinafter together referred to as superior for the purposes of this Section) if a person under their effective subordination and control or effective authority and control committed a crime defined in this Chapter, and the superior knew or, in the circumstances, should have known about the commission of this crime or its preparation, and did not take the measures within their competence necessary to prevent the crime, or did not file a complaint without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the crime;
b) an official or foreign official in a leading position not falling under point a) (hereinafter referred to as official leader for the purposes of this Section) if a person under their effective authority and control (hereinafter referred to as subordinate for the purposes of this Section) committed a crime defined in this Chapter as a result of their failure to exercise proper control over them, if
ba) they knew that their subordinate committed or was preparing such a crime, or consciously disregarded circumstances clearly indicating this,
bb) the crime concerned activities falling within their effective duties or competence, and
bc) they did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within their competence to prevent its commission, or did not file a complaint without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the crime.
14

## 6. §

(1) Whoever recruits persons in the territory of Hungary for military service or other service of military interest in a foreign armed organisation – other than allied armed forces – or acts as an intermediary for such service, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) A Hungarian citizen who voluntarily joins, offers to join, or participates in training in a foreign armed organisation – other than allied armed forces – participating in an international or non-international armed conflict shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) If the person recruited or intermediated in the territory of Hungary for military service or other service of military interest in a foreign armed organisation has not reached the age of eighteen, the recruiter or intermediary shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.
14

## 7. §

(1) Whoever violates the conditions of an armistice shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the violation of the armistice leads to particularly serious consequences.
14

## 8. §

(1) Anyone who assaults, unlawfully detains, or applies other violence against an enemy's military envoy or their escort, if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Anyone who kills the military envoy or their escort shall be punishable by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.
14

## 9. § (1) Anyone who during wartime

a) directs an attack against protected persons, or
b) directs an attack which may cause death or serious injury to protected persons, knowing that it is clearly excessive in relation to the anticipated direct military advantage,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.
(2) Anyone who during wartime
a) kills a protected person,
b) forces a protected person into living conditions that threaten the protected person with destruction,
c) forces a protected person to sexual violence or to tolerate it, to prostitution, to carry a foetus to term or to abortion,
d) unlawfully deprives a protected person of their personal liberty,
e) deports or abducts a protected person from their lawful place of residence, except where justified by important military necessity,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.
(3) Anyone who during wartime violates the right of a protected person to self-determination in healthcare, or causes serious physical or mental harm to the protected person, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.
(4) Anyone who during wartime deprives a protected person or a group of protected persons of the right to a fair and regular judicial procedure shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.
(5) Anyone who commits preparation for the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.
15

## 0. §

Anyone who during wartime gives an order or instruction to their subordinate to leave no survivors, if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
15

## 1. §

(1) Anyone who uses a protected person to shield a particular area or military forces from enemy military operations shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.
(2) Anyone who commits preparation for the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.
15

## 2. § (1) Anyone who

a) compels a protected person to serve in the armed forces or group of a power hostile to them,
b) compels a national of the enemy power to participate in military operations against their own state,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.
(2) Anyone who commits preparation for the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.
(3) Anyone who conscripts, enlists a person who has not attained the age of eighteen into armed forces or a group, or induces them to participate in war, provides assistance for this, or ensures the conditions necessary for this, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.
15

## 3. §

(1) Anyone who during wartime launches or continues an attack against an installation that does not qualify as a military objective and is militarily undefended, or launches or continues an attack which causes damage in a militarily undefended installation or may cause widespread, long-term and severe damage to the natural environment which is clearly excessive in relation to the anticipated direct military advantage, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to ten years if the attack is directed against
a) a hospital, a place for the treatment or accommodation of the sick and wounded, or
b) cultural property protected by international treaty.
(3) Anyone who uses or employs cultural property protected by international treaty or their immediate surroundings for military purposes, or appropriates, plunders, damages or destroys such property, shall be punishable according to paragraph (2).
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the criminal offence defined in paragraph (2) point b) or paragraph (3) is committed against cultural property under special or enhanced protection according to international treaty, or their immediate surroundings.
(5) For the purposes of this Section
a) cultural property: cultural property as defined in Article 1 of the international Convention for the Protection of Cultural Property in the Event of Armed Conflict, concluded at The Hague on 14 May 1954, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 14 of 1957,
b) cultural property under special protection: cultural property as defined in Article 8 of the Convention referred to in point a),
c) cultural property under enhanced protection: cultural property as defined in Article 10 of the Second Protocol to the Convention referred to in point a), promulgated by Act XXIX of 2006.
15

## 4. § (1) Anyone who during wartime in an operational or occupied territory *

a) plunders civilian property,
b) deprives the population of property necessary for their subsistence by extorting services or by other means, or destroys such property, except where justified by important military necessity,
if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) Anyone who plunders the fallen, the wounded or the sick on the battlefield shall be punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.
15

## 5. §

(1) * Anyone who during wartime in an operational or occupied territory employs or causes to be employed a weapon or means of warfare prohibited by international treaty shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from ten to twenty years or by life imprisonment.
(2) Anyone who commits preparation for the criminal offence defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.
15

## 6. §

Anyone who during wartime directs an attack against the personnel, equipment, means, units or vehicles of a humanitarian, relief or peacekeeping organization operating in accordance with the Charter of the United Nations, if they are entitled to the protection afforded to protected persons or installations by the international law of armed conflict, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.
15

## 7. §

Anyone who during wartime abuses the emblem of the red cross, the red crescent or the red crystal, or other emblem or sign serving a similar purpose and protected by international law, if a more serious criminal offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
15

## 8. §

Other war crimes and the punishments that may be imposed for their commission are provided for by the provisions of Decree No. 81/1945. (II. 5.) ME, which was elevated to the force of law by Act VII of 1945, as amended and supplemented by Decree No. 1440/1945. (V. 1.) ME.
15

## 9. § A person is liable in the same manner as the perpetrator of an offence defined in this chapter

a) a military superior or a person acting as a military commander (hereinafter together referred to as: superior, for the purposes of this §), if a person under his or her effective subordination and control, or effective authority and control, committed an offence defined in this chapter, and the superior knew or, in the circumstances, should have known about the commission of this offence or its preparation, and did not take the necessary measures within his or her competence to prevent the offence, or did not make a report without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the offence;
b) an official or foreign official person in a leading position not falling under point a) (hereinafter referred to as: official leader, for the purposes of this §) as well, if a person under his or her effective authority and control (hereinafter referred to as: subordinate, for the purposes of this §) committed an offence defined in this chapter, as a result of failing to exercise proper control over him or her, if
ba) he or she knew that his or her subordinate committed or was preparing such an offence, or consciously disregarded circumstances clearly indicating this,
bb) the offence affected activities falling within his or her effective duties or competence, and
bc) he or she did not take all necessary and reasonable measures within his or her competence to prevent its commission, or did not make a report without delay after becoming aware of the commission of the offence.
16

## 0. §

(1) A person who kills another is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from ten to twenty years or life imprisonment if the homicide is committed
a) with premeditation,
b) out of greed,
c) from or for a base motive or purpose,
d) with particular cruelty,
e) against an official person or a foreign official person, during or because of his or her official proceedings, against a person performing a public duty, during the performance of this duty, and against a person who came to the support or defence of the official, foreign official or person performing a public duty,
f) against multiple persons,
g) endangering the life of multiple persons,
h) as a special recidivist,
i) against a person who has not reached the age of fourteen,
j) * against a person incapable of defence,
k) * against a person with limited capacity to avert the offence due to his or her old age or disability, or
l) * after an indictment has been brought against the perpetrator for homicide.
(3) A person who commits preparation for homicide is punishable by imprisonment from one to five years.
(4) A person who commits homicide by negligence is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment from one to five years.
(5) A person is punishable under subsection (1) who incites a person who has not reached the age of fourteen or a person incapable of expressing his or her will to commit suicide, if the suicide is committed.
(6) For the purposes of point h) of subsection (2), offences of a similar nature for the purposes of special recidivism are
a) genocide [14

## 2. §

subsection (1) point a)], homicide committed in strong emotion (16

## 1. §),

b) the more severely qualified cases of kidnapping and violence against a superior or service member [19

## 0. §

subsection (4), 44

## 5. § subsection (5) point a)],

c) the more severely qualified cases of terrorist act, seizure of control of a vehicle, and mutiny, if committed by intentionally causing death [31

## 4. §

subsection (1), 32

## 0. §

subsection (2), 44

## 2. § subsection (4)]

(7) * Point l) of subsection (2) is applicable if the perpetrator
a) is convicted in the same proceedings for the homicide committed after the indictment and for the homicide committed earlier, or
b) has been convicted with final effect for the homicide committed earlier.
16

## 1. §

A person who kills another in strong emotion arising from an excusable cause is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.
16

## 2. §

(1) A person who incites another to commit suicide, or provides assistance for its commission, if the suicide is attempted or committed, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) A person who has reached the age of eighteen who incites a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to commit suicide, or provides assistance for its commission, if the suicide is attempted or committed, is punishable by imprisonment from two to eight years.
16

## 3. §

(1) A person who aborts another's foetus is punishable for a felony by imprisonment up to three years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the abortion is committed
a) as a business,
b) without the consent of the pregnant woman, or
c) causing serious bodily injury or danger to life.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the abortion causes the death of the pregnant woman.
(4) A woman who aborts or has her foetus aborted is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to one year.
16

## 4. § (1) A person who injures another's bodily integrity or health commits bodily injury

(2) If the injury or illness caused by the bodily injury heals within eight days, the perpetrator is punishable for the misdemeanour of minor bodily injury by imprisonment up to two years.
(3) If the injury or illness caused by the bodily injury heals beyond eight days, the perpetrator is punishable for the felony of serious bodily injury by imprisonment up to three years.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if the minor bodily injury is committed
a) from or for a base motive or purpose,
b) against a person incapable of defence or of expressing his or her will, or
c) against a person with limited capacity to avert the offence due to his or her old age or disability.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the minor bodily injury causes permanent disability or serious deterioration of health.
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if the serious bodily injury is committed
a) from or for a base motive or purpose,
b) against a person incapable of defence or of expressing his or her will,
c) against a person with limited capacity to avert the offence due to his or her old age or disability,
d) causing permanent disability or serious deterioration of health,
e) with particular cruelty.
(7) A person who commits preparation for the offence defined in subsection (3) or (6) is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment up to one year.
(8) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the bodily injury causes danger to life or death.
(9) A person who commits serious bodily injury by negligence is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment
a) in the case defined in subsection (3), up to one year,
b) in the case defined in points b)–c) of subsection (6), up to three years,
c) in case of causing life-threatening injury, from one to five years.
(10) The offence defined in subsection (2) is punishable only upon private motion.
16

## 5. §

(1) A person who, by violating an occupational rule, negligently exposes the life, physical integrity or health of another person or other persons to direct danger, or causes bodily harm, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(2) The punishment shall be
a) imprisonment not exceeding three years, if the offence causes permanent disability, serious deterioration of health or mass disaster,
b) imprisonment from one year to five years, if the offence causes death,
c) imprisonment from two years to eight years, if the offence causes the death of more than two persons, or causes a fatal mass disaster.
(3) If the perpetrator causes the direct danger intentionally, he or she shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years in the case defined in paragraph (1), and in the case defined in paragraph (2) – according to the distinctions made therein – by imprisonment from one year to five years, from two years to eight years, or from five years to ten years.
(4) * For the purposes of this Section, occupational rule shall also include the rules relating to the use and handling of functional firearms, explosives and explosive materials.
16

## 6. §

(1) A person who does not provide assistance that can be expected of him or her to an injured person or to a person whose life or physical integrity is in direct danger shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the victim dies and the provision of assistance could have saved his or her life.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony in the case of paragraph (1), and imprisonment from one year to five years in the case of paragraph (2), if the dangerous situation was caused by the perpetrator, or if he or she is otherwise obliged to provide assistance.
(4) The last clause of paragraph (3) shall not be applicable to a person who is obliged to provide assistance under traffic rules.
16

## 7. §

A person who fails to fulfil his or her care obligation towards a person who, due to his or her condition or old age, is unable to care for himself or herself, and thereby endangers the life, physical integrity or health of the person in need of care, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
16

## 8. §

(1) A person who performs an intervention on the human or foetal genome, or on the genome of a human embryo, aimed at altering it, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the intervention referred to in paragraph (1) causes an alteration of the genome.
16

## 9. §

(1) A person who uses germ cells derived from a deceased person or a deceased foetus for a special procedure aimed at human reproduction shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) A person who commits a preparatory act aimed at the prohibited use of human germ cells shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
17

## 0. §

A person who performs an intervention aimed at selecting the sex of the child to be born shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
17

## 1. §

A person who conducts medical research on a human being without authorisation or in deviation from the authorisation shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
17

## 2. §

(1) A person who conducts medical research on human germ cells or on a human embryo without authorisation or in deviation from the authorisation, or creates a human embryo for research purposes, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) A person who
a) transplants a human embryo into an animal organism,
b) fertilises human and animal germ cells with each other,
c) implants into a human organism a human embryo on which research has been conducted,
d) uses human germ cells used for research for human reproduction,
e) uses non-human germ cells or embryos for human fertilisation or embryo implantation,
f) uses a human embryo to create multiple human embryos or animal embryos,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years.
(3) A person who commits a preparatory act aimed at the offence defined in paragraph (2) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(4) For the purposes of this Section, human germ cells shall also include foetal germ cells.
17

## 3. §

(1) A person who conducts research on a human embryo aimed at altering the genome of the embryo shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) A person who
a) uses a human embryo to create an individual with characteristics different from or additional to those established at conception, or
b) separates the cells of a human embryo,
shall be punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years.
(3) A person who commits a preparatory act aimed at the offence defined in paragraph (2) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
17

## 4. §

(1) A person who creates genetically identical human individuals in the course of medical research or intervention shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five years to ten years.
(2) A person who commits a preparatory act aimed at the offence defined in paragraph (1) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
17

## 5. §

(1) A person who unlawfully obtains, places into circulation for pecuniary gain, or trades in human genes, cells, germ cells, embryos, organs, tissues, the body of a deceased person or parts thereof, or a deceased foetus, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the prohibited use of the human body is committed by an employee of a healthcare provider in the course of his or her occupation.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years in the case defined in paragraph (1), and imprisonment from two years to eight years in the case defined in paragraph (2), if the prohibited use of the human body is committed
a) against a person under eighteen years of age,
b) as a business, or
c) in a criminal organisation.
(4) A person who commits a preparatory act aimed at the prohibited use of the human body shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year in the case defined in paragraph (1), and by imprisonment not exceeding two years in the cases defined in paragraphs (2) and (3).
(5) For the purposes of this Section, embryo shall also include an embryo removed from the mother's body, as well as an embryo created or produced during a special procedure aimed at human reproduction and not implanted in the uterus.
17

## 6. §

(1) A person who offers, transfers, places into circulation, or trades in narcotic drugs shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two years to eight years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as an official or a person performing a public duty, using this capacity, or
c) in the premises of the Hungarian Defence Forces, law enforcement agencies or the National Tax and Customs Administration.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to twenty years or life imprisonment if the offence is committed in relation to a significant quantity of narcotic drugs.
(4) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(3).
(5) A person who offers or transfers a small quantity of narcotic drugs shall be punishable
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1) for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years,
b) in the cases defined in paragraph (2) points b)–c) by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(6) A person who
a) commits a preparatory act aimed at the offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years,
b) commits a preparatory act aimed at the offence defined in paragraph (3) shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years.
17

## 7. § (1) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who

a) offers or transfers narcotics to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen,
b) places narcotics on the market or trades in them using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen,
c) on the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity
ca) offers or transfers narcotics,
cb) places narcotics on the market or trades in them,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if the offence is committed
a) in respect of a significant quantity of narcotics,
b) in a criminal association,
c) as a person performing official or public duties, exploiting this capacity.
(3) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2).
(4) A person who commits the offence specified in paragraph (1) point a) or ca) in respect of a small quantity of narcotics shall be punishable by imprisonment from one to five years, or from two to eight years if committed as a person performing official or public duties, exploiting this capacity.
(5) A person who commits preparation for the commission of the offence specified in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
17

## 8. §

(1) A person who cultivates, produces, obtains, possesses, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, or transports through it narcotics shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) The punishment shall be
a) imprisonment from two to eight years if
aa) committed as a business,
ab) in a criminal association,
ac) as a person performing official or public duties, exploiting this capacity,
b) imprisonment from five to ten years if committed in respect of a significant quantity of narcotics,
c) imprisonment from five to fifteen years if committed in respect of a particularly significant quantity of narcotics.
(3) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2).
(4) A person who commits preparation for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(5) If the offence is committed in respect of a small quantity of narcotics, the punishment shall be
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2) points aa) and ac), imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(6) A person who consumes narcotics, or obtains or possesses a small quantity of narcotics for the purpose of consumption, if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
17

## 9. § (1) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who

a) using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen, or
b) on the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity
cultivates, produces, obtains or possesses narcotics, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.
(2) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, or transports through it narcotics using a person who has not reached the age of eighteen shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) The punishment shall be
a) imprisonment from five to ten years if
aa) in a criminal association,
ab) as a business,
ac) as a person performing official or public duties, exploiting this capacity,
b) imprisonment from five to fifteen years if committed in respect of a significant quantity of narcotics,
c) imprisonment from five to twenty years or life imprisonment if committed in respect of a particularly significant quantity of narcotics.
(4) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(3).
(5) A person who commits preparation for the commission of the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(6) * If the offence is committed in respect of a small quantity of narcotics, the punishment shall be for a felony
a) in the case specified in paragraphs (1) and (2), imprisonment not exceeding three years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (3) points ab) and ac), imprisonment from one to five years.
18

## 0. §

(1) * A person who cultivates, produces, obtains or possesses a small quantity of narcotics for personal use, or who consumes narcotics, shall not be punishable if he or she admits the commission of the offence and proves by document by the time of the first-instance decision on the merits that he or she has received continuous treatment for narcotic addiction, other care treating narcotic use, or has participated in preventive-educational services for at least six months.
(2) * Paragraph (1) shall not apply if, within two years prior to the commission of the offence,
a) the proceedings against the perpetrator were suspended in view of the ground for terminating punishability specified in paragraph (1), or
b) the perpetrator's criminal liability was established for drug trafficking or drug possession.
(3) * In the case of 17

## 8. §

paragraphs (1) and (5)–(6), and 17

## 9. §

paragraphs (1)–(2) and (6) – if paragraph (1) is not applicable – the punishment may be reduced without limitation if the perpetrator makes it possible to establish the identity of the person selling the narcotics by the time of the indictment. This provision shall not apply if the offence is committed in a criminal organisation.
18

## 1. § (1) A person who has reached the age of eighteen and who

a) induces or attempts to induce a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to the pathological enjoyment of an intoxicating substance or agent not classified as a narcotic, or
b) attempts to induce a person who has not reached the age of eighteen to consume narcotics,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) If a more serious offence is not committed, a person who has reached the age of eighteen and who provides assistance to a person who has not reached the age of eighteen for the pathological enjoyment of an intoxicating substance or agent not classified as a narcotic shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
18

## 2. §

(1) A person who, for the purpose of cultivating or producing narcotics,
a) manufactures, obtains, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, transports through it, or transfers the necessary substance, equipment or apparatus,
b) places on the market or trades in the necessary substance, equipment or apparatus,
if a more serious offence is not committed, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) A person who provides material resources for the commission of the offence shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if
a) the offence specified in paragraph (1) is committed in a criminal association, or
b) the offence specified in paragraph (1) point a) is committed as a business.
(4) A person who commits preparation for the offence specified in paragraph (1) or (3) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(5) A person shall not be punishable who – before his or her activity facilitating the preparation of narcotics came to the knowledge of the authorities – discloses his or her act to the authorities, hands over the substance, equipment or apparatus in his or her possession to the authorities, and makes it possible to establish the identity of other persons participating in facilitating the preparation of narcotics.
18

## 3. §

(1) Whoever, in relation to a drug precursor defined in a legal act of the European Union,
a) possesses, places on the market, transports it, or carries out intermediary activity with it without authorisation or exceeding the limits of the authorisation,
b) obtains it by making a declaration with false content for that purpose,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Whoever violates the notification obligation in connection with the placing on the market, transport of, or intermediary activity carried out with a drug precursor defined in a legal act of the European Union shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(3) For the purposes of this §,
a) legal act of the European Union means Regulation (EC) No 273/2004 on drug precursors (hereinafter in this paragraph: Regulation (EC) No 273/2004), and Regulation (EC) No 111/2005 laying down rules for the monitoring of trade between the Community and third countries in drug precursors (hereinafter in this paragraph: Regulation (EC) No 111/2005),
b) placing on the market means the concept defined in Article 2(c) of Regulation (EC) No 273/2004,
c) transport means the concept defined in Article 2(c) and (d) of Regulation (EC) No 111/2005,
d) intermediary activity means the concept defined in Article 2(e) of Regulation (EC) No 111/2005,
e) notification obligation means the obligation defined in Article 8(1) of Regulation (EC) No 273/2004 and in Article 9(1) of Regulation (EC) No 111/2005.
18

## 4. §

* (1) Whoever offers, transfers, places on the market or trades in a new psychoactive substance shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as an official or a person performing a public duty, using that capacity, or
c) in the premises of the Hungarian Defence Forces, law enforcement agencies or the National Tax and Customs Administration.
(2a) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.
(3) * Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2a) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2a).
(4) Whoever offers or transfers a small quantity of new psychoactive substance shall be punishable
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1) for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year,
b) in the case defined in paragraph (2)(b)–(c) for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(5) * Whoever commits preparation for the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2a) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
184/A. § * (1) A person who has attained the age of eighteen years and who
a) offers or transfers a new psychoactive substance to a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years,
b) places on the market or trades in a new psychoactive substance using a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years,
c) in the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity,
ca) offers or transfers a new psychoactive substance,
cb) places on the market or trades in a new psychoactive substance,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two years to eight years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as an official or a person performing a public duty, using that capacity,
c) * in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.
(3) Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2).
(4) Whoever commits the offence defined in paragraph (1)(a) or paragraph (1)(c)(ca) in relation to a small quantity of new psychoactive substance shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years, or in case of commission as an official or a person performing a public duty, using that capacity, by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(5) Whoever commits preparation for the commission of the offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
184/B. § * (1) Whoever
a) produces, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it or transports through it a new psychoactive substance,
b) obtains or possesses a new psychoactive substance exceeding a small quantity,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the offence is committed
a) as a business,
b) in a criminal organisation,
c) as an official or a person performing a public duty, using that capacity.
(2a) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence is committed in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.
(2b) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed in relation to a particularly significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.
(3) * Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2b).
(4) * Whoever commits preparation for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(5) If the offence is committed in relation to a small quantity of new psychoactive substance, the punishment shall be
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1)(a) for a misdemeanour imprisonment not exceeding one year,
b) in the case defined in paragraph (2)(a) and (c) for a misdemeanour imprisonment not exceeding two years.
184/C. § * (1) A person who has attained the age of eighteen years and who
a) using a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years
aa) produces, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it or transports through it a new psychoactive substance,
ab) obtains or possesses a new psychoactive substance exceeding a small quantity,
b) in the premises of a building designated for educational, public education, child welfare or child protection tasks, or in its immediate vicinity
ba) produces a new psychoactive substance,
bb) obtains or possesses a new psychoactive substance exceeding a small quantity,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as a business,
c) as an official or a person performing a public duty, using that capacity.
(2a) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence is committed in relation to a significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.
(2b) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the offence is committed in relation to a particularly significant quantity of new psychoactive substance.
(3) * Whoever provides material means for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2b).
(4) * Whoever commits preparation for the commission of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(2b) shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(5) If the offence is committed in relation to a small quantity of new psychoactive substance, the punishment shall be
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1)(a)(aa) and paragraph (1)(b)(ba) for a misdemeanour imprisonment not exceeding two years,
b) in the case defined in paragraph (2)(b) and (c) for a felony imprisonment not exceeding three years.
184/D. § *
18

## 5. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of enhancing sports performance, in relation to a prohibited performance-enhancing substance
a) manufactures,
b) offers, transfers, places on the market,
c) prescribes on a medical prescription or veterinary prescription,
is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the offence is committed on a commercial basis or in a criminal organisation.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if
a) as a result of the offence, a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years obtains a prohibited performance-enhancing substance,
b) a person who has attained the age of eighteen years commits the offence defined in paragraph (1) points a) or b) by using a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years.
(4) Whoever commits preparation for the abuse of a performance-enhancing substance defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(5) A person who has attained the age of eighteen years who induces a person who has not attained the age of eighteen years to use a prohibited performance-enhancing substance, or provides assistance to such person for the use of a prohibited performance-enhancing substance, is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(6) * For the purposes of this Section, a prohibited performance-enhancing substance means: any substance which, in view of its active ingredient, belongs to the category of anabolic agents, peptide hormones, growth factors and related compounds, hormone antagonists and modulators, and is listed by name in the government decree promulgating the international convention against doping in sport or its amendment.
185/A. § * (1) Whoever
a) falsifies a medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product, or manufactures a counterfeit medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product,
b) places on the market or trades in a counterfeit, falsified or medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product not authorised in Hungary, or offers, transfers it in an unjustified quantity,
c) imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, transports through it, or acquires, possesses in an unjustified quantity a counterfeit or falsified medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product,
d) acquires, possesses in an unjustified quantity, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, transports through it a medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product not authorised in Hungary,
e) uses an original document related to a medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product for commercial purposes in a manner contrary to its intended purpose,
is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) * If the offence defined in paragraph (1) is committed in relation to a medicinal product containing a psychotropic substance listed in Schedule 3 or 4 of psychotropic substances in Annex 2 to Government Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.) of the Ministry of Interior on controlled substances [hereinafter: BM Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.)], or in relation to the active ingredient of such a medicinal product in breach of the notification obligation, without authorisation or exceeding the limits of the authorisation, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years.
(3) The punishment in the cases defined in paragraph (1) points a) and b) is
a) imprisonment from one year to five years if the offence causes permanent disability or serious deterioration of health,
b) imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence causes death.
(4) The punishment in the case defined in paragraph (2) is
a) imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence under paragraph (1) point a) or b) causes permanent disability or serious deterioration of health,
b) imprisonment from five years to ten years if the offence under paragraph (1) point a) or b) causes death.
(5) Whoever commits the offence
a) as a healthcare professional,
b) as an employee of an organisation authorised to manufacture, wholesale, or provide direct supply to the public, or
c) in a criminal organisation,
is punishable in the case defined in paragraph (1) by imprisonment from one year to five years, and in the case defined in paragraph (2) by imprisonment from two years to eight years.
(6) The punishment is as provided in paragraph (5) if the counterfeit, falsified or medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product not authorised in Hungary becomes widely accessible to users.
(7) Whoever commits preparation for the falsification of medicinal products defined in paragraphs (1)–(6) is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(8) For the purposes of this Section
a) an unjustified quantity means a quantity which can be established as not being intended to satisfy the personal needs of a specific person,
b) a medicinal product or veterinary medicinal product not authorised in Hungary also means a product in which a pharmaceutical active ingredient is used in breach of the legal provisions on the composition of the product in question. A medicinal product not holding a marketing authorisation in Hungary shall also be considered an authorised medicinal product if conduct defined in paragraph (1) point b) or d) is carried out in relation to it which may be lawfully performed in possession of an official authorisation prescribed by law or following notification.
18

## 6. § (1) Whoever

a) falsifies a medical device, or manufactures a counterfeit medical device,
b) offers, transfers, places on the market, or trades in a counterfeit, falsified or medical device not authorised in Hungary,
c) imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, transports through it, or acquires, possesses in an unjustified quantity a counterfeit or falsified medical device,
d) acquires, possesses in an unjustified quantity, imports into the territory of the country, exports from it, transports through it a medical device not authorised in Hungary,
e) uses an original document related to a medical device for commercial purposes in a manner contrary to its intended purpose,
is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment in the cases defined in paragraph (1) points a) and b) is
a) imprisonment from one year to five years if the offence causes permanent disability or serious deterioration of health,
b) imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offence causes death.
(3) Whoever commits the offence defined in paragraph (1)
a) as a healthcare professional,
b) as an employee of an organisation authorised to manufacture, wholesale, or provide direct supply to the public, or
c) in a criminal organisation,
is punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(4) The punishment is as provided in paragraph (3) if the counterfeit, falsified or medical device not authorised in Hungary becomes widely accessible to users.
(4a) * Whoever commits preparation for the falsification of a medical device defined in paragraphs (1)–(4) is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(5) For the purposes of this Section
a) * medical device means: a medical device, an in vitro diagnostic medical device and an investigational medicinal product;
b) an unjustified quantity means a quantity which can be established as not being intended to satisfy the personal needs of a specific person;
c) * a medical device not authorised in Hungary also means a medical device placed on the market without carrying out the conformity assessment procedure.
18

## 7. § (1) * Whoever for remuneration or regularly

a) medical practice,
b) *
c) activities falling within the scope of non-conventional healing and naturopathic procedures
unlawfully carries out, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
