a) * the concepts defined in the Act on the general rules on the entry and residence of third-country nationals shall be understood as particularly exploitative working conditions;
b) significant number: at least five persons.
35

## 7. §

(1) Whoever damages a monument, an object belonging to the category of protected cultural property or an archaeological site located on his or her own property shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Whoever
a) destroys a monument or an object belonging to the category of protected cultural property in his or her ownership,
b) causes irreparable damage to a monument as a result of which it loses its monumental character, or
c) causes irreparable damage to an object belonging to the category of protected cultural property or to an archaeological site
shall be punishable by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(3) * Whoever commits the offence specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) in relation to a foreign protected cultural heritage element in his or her ownership shall be punishable in accordance with paragraphs (1)–(2).
35

## 8. § (1) Whoever

a) alienates an object belonging to a protected collection or a piece of a protected group of objects without the prior consent prescribed by law,
b) fails to comply with the obligation prescribed by law to notify changes in the ownership of an object, collection or group of objects belonging to the category of protected cultural property,
c) transfers abroad an object, collection or group of objects belonging to the category of protected cultural property without authorisation, or exceeds the limits of the export authorisation,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Whoever transfers abroad an object belonging to the category of cultural property and subject to export authorisation without authorisation, or exceeds the limits of the export authorisation, shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
35

## 9. § (1) Whoever

a) keeps, breeds, alienates or brings into the territory of the country a dangerous animal without authorisation,
b) violates the safety regulations prescribed by law for the keeping of dangerous animals,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) Whoever
a) with regard to a dangerous dog
aa) breeds, brings into or takes out of the territory of the country, competes, or keeps without authorisation,
ab) alienates or acquires in violation of the law, or
b) with regard to a dangerous dog
ba) violates the obligation prescribed by law for neutering, or
bb) violates the safety regulations prescribed by law for keeping
shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) Whoever keeps, trains a dangerous dog for the performance of guarding and protection tasks, or performs such tasks with a dangerous dog, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(4) For the purposes of this §, a dangerous dog is a dog individually declared a dangerous dog by an official decision.
36

## 0. § * Whoever

a) regularly organises prohibited gambling,
b) makes premises or equipment available for the regular organisation of prohibited gambling, or
c) invites participation in prohibited gambling before a large public,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
36

## 1. § Whoever *

a) violates the rules of epidemiological isolation, observation, quarantine or control ordered to prevent the introduction or spread of an infectious disease subject to quarantine obligation,
b) during an epidemic violates the rules of the ordered epidemiological isolation, observation, quarantine or control,
c) violates the rules of plant health or animal epidemic measures ordered to prevent or eliminate the introduction and export, as well as the spread of infectious animal diseases or plant quarantine pests,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by confinement.
36

## 2. § Whoever in the European Union

a) illegally brings into the territory of the country, stores, transports, places on the market or releases into the environment propagating material of a plant variety modified by genetic engineering for which no authorisation exists,
b) illegally releases into the environment propagating material of a plant variety modified by genetic engineering for which authorisation does not extend to use for cultivation purposes,
c) violates the prohibition on import, production, storage, transport, placing on the market and use ordered as a safeguard measure for the duration of the safeguard procedure in respect of propagating material of a plant variety modified by genetic engineering for which authorisation exists for cultivation purposes,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
36

## 3. §

(1) Whoever violates his or her obligation to cooperate as defined in the Act on the State Audit Office during an audit by the State Audit Office, and thereby obstructs the conduct of the audit, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) The head of the audited organisation shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1) if, despite a written request, he or she does not send an action plan to the State Audit Office.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the act specified in paragraph (1) frustrates the conduct of the audit.
36

## 4. §

* Expulsion may also be imposed on the perpetrator of people smuggling, illegal crossing of the border barrier, damaging the border barrier, obstructing construction work related to the border barrier, facilitating illegal residence, facilitating and supporting illegal immigration, and organising prohibited gambling.
36

## 5. §

(1) Whoever takes away another's property from another person for the purpose of unlawful appropriation by
a) using violence against someone, or direct threat against life or physical integrity, or
b) placing them in an unconscious or defenseless state,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two to eight years.
(2) Robbery also occurs when a thief caught in the act uses violence or direct threat against life or physical integrity for the purpose of retaining the property.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if the robbery is committed
a) while armed,
b) while equipped with weapons,
c) in a group,
d) in a criminal association,
e) for significant value,
f) against an official person, foreign official person or person performing public duties, during their official proceedings or performance of their duties,
g) against a person with limited capacity to recognize or prevent the crime due to their old age or disability.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the robbery is committed
a) for particularly high value or exceeding that,
b) for significant value in the manner specified in points a)–d) of paragraph (3),
c) against an official person, foreign official person or person performing public duties, during their official proceedings or performance of their duties, in the manner specified in points a)–d) of paragraph (3).
(5) Whoever commits preparation for robbery shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment up to two years.
36

## 6. § (1) Whoever takes away another's property for the purpose of unlawful appropriation

a) by taking it from another person by intoxicating them or inducing their stupefied state for this purpose,
b) by taking it from a person who is under the effect of violence or direct threat against life or physical integrity applied by them during the commission of another crime, or
c) by taking it from a person who is defenseless or has limited capacity to recognize or prevent the crime due to their old age or disability,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the plundering is committed
a) for significant value,
b) in a group,
c) in a criminal association.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if the plundering is committed
a) for particularly high value or exceeding that,
b) for significant value, in a group or in a criminal association.
36

## 7. §

(1) Whoever compels another person by violence or threat to do, not do or tolerate something for the purpose of unlawful gain, and thereby causes financial disadvantage, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the extortion is committed
a) in a criminal association,
b) with threat against life or physical integrity, or other similarly serious threat,
c) as an official person using this capacity,
d) by pretending official mandate or capacity.
36

## 8. §

(1) Whoever compels another person by violence or threat to do, not do or tolerate something for the purpose of enforcing a lawful or allegedly lawful financial claim shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the vigilantism is committed
a) while armed,
b) while equipped with weapons,
c) in a group,
d) against a defenseless person.
(3) Vigilantism does not occur if the use of violence or threat is a permitted means of enforcing the claim.
36

## 9. §

For the purposes of this chapter, for the purposes of special recidivism, similar offenses are violent crimes against property and crimes against property.
37

## 0. §

* (1) Whoever takes away another's property from another person in order to unlawfully appropriate it commits theft.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment up to two years for a misdemeanor if
a) the theft is committed for lesser value or
b) the theft committed for petty offense value is committed
ba) in a criminal association,
bb) habitually,
bc) with violence against property – including when a device serving to prevent the appropriation of the property is removed without causing damage to its substance, or is rendered incapable of preventing the appropriation of the property –,
bd) by pickpocketing,
be) with the simultaneous taking of one or more public documents, private documents or cash substitute payment instruments,
bf) by entering a premises or enclosed area belonging to it by deception, or without the knowledge and consent of the entitled person or user,
bg) by using a false or stolen key,
bh) against a victim sharing a dwelling or similar premises with the perpetrator, or
bi) by illegal tree felling in a forest.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if
a) the theft is committed for higher value,
b) the theft committed for lesser value is committed
ba) in one of the manners specified in points ba)–be) of paragraph (2),
bb) * for an object belonging to protected cultural assets, archaeological find or foreign protected movable cultural heritage element,
bc) for an object of religious worship,
bd) for an object on a corpse, or for an object dedicated to the memory of the deceased in a cemetery or memorial site,
be) for precious metal, or
c) the theft is committed for petty offense or lesser value at the scene of public danger.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if
a) the theft is committed for significant value, or
b) the theft committed for higher value is committed in one of the manners specified in points ba)–be) of paragraph (2) or at the scene of public danger.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if
a) the theft is committed for particularly high value, or
b) the theft committed for significant value is committed in one of the manners specified in points ba)–be) of paragraph (2) or at the scene of public danger.
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if
a) the theft is committed for particularly significant value, or
b) the theft committed for particularly high value is committed in one of the manners specified in points ba)–be) of paragraph (2) or at the scene of public danger.
37

## 1. §

(1) Whoever causes damage by destroying or damaging another's property commits vandalism.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment up to one year for a misdemeanor if
a) the vandalism causes lesser damage, or
b) the vandalism causing damage not exceeding the petty offense value threshold is committed
ba) by placing graffiti, or
bb) in a criminal association.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment up to three years for a felony if
a) the vandalism causes higher damage,
b) the perpetrator damages
ba) * an object belonging to protected cultural assets, a monument, archaeological site, archaeological find or foreign protected cultural heritage element,
bb) * an object of religious worship or a church, other place designated for religious practice,
bc) a burial place, memorial site, or an object dedicated to the memory of the deceased in a cemetery or memorial site.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from one to five years if
a) the vandalism causes significant damage,
b) the perpetrator destroys an object, building or place specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (3),
c) the vandalism is committed using explosives or explosive devices.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the vandalism causes particularly high damage.
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if the vandalism causes particularly significant damage.
(7) For the purposes of this §, graffiti means: a pictorial, graphic or textual surface coating created with spray paint, marker pen or any other surface-forming material, which is not necessary for the proper use of the property.
37

## 2. §

(1) Whoever unlawfully misappropriates property of another entrusted to them, or disposes of it as their own, commits embezzlement.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour, if
a) the embezzlement is committed for a lesser value or
b) the embezzlement committed for a petty offence value is committed
ba) in a criminal association,
bb) at the scene of a public danger,
bc) on a commercial basis.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony, if
a) the embezzlement is committed for a greater value,
b) the embezzlement committed for a lesser value is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2), or
c) * the embezzlement is committed in respect of an object belonging to the category of protected cultural property, an archaeological find or a movable cultural heritage element under foreign protection.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years, if
a) the embezzlement is committed for a significant value,
b) the embezzlement committed for a greater value is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2), or
c) the embezzlement is committed to the detriment of a person with limited capacity to recognise or avert the offence due to their advanced age or disability.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years, if
a) the embezzlement is committed for a particularly large value or
b) the embezzlement committed for a significant value is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2).
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years, if
a) the embezzlement is committed for a particularly significant value or
b) the embezzlement committed for a particularly large value is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2).
37

## 3. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of unlawful gain, misleads another or keeps another in error, and thereby causes damage, commits fraud.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour, if
a) the fraud causes lesser damage, or
b) the fraud causing damage not exceeding the petty offence value threshold is committed
ba) in a criminal association,
bb) at the scene of a public danger,
bc) on a commercial basis,
bd) by simulating a charitable fundraising campaign.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony, if
a) the fraud causes greater damage, or
b) the fraud causing lesser damage is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2).
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years, if
a) the fraud causes significant damage,
b) the fraud causing greater damage is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2), or
c) the fraud is committed to the detriment of a person with limited capacity to recognise or avert the offence due to their advanced age or disability.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years, if
a) the fraud causes particularly large damage, or
b) the fraud causing significant damage is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2).
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years, if
a) the fraud causes particularly significant damage, or
b) the fraud causing particularly large damage is committed in one of the ways specified in points ba)–bc) of paragraph (2).
(7) For the purposes of this §, the unpaid consideration for a service used shall also be regarded as damage.
37

## 4. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of unlawful gain, carries out simulated economic activity and thereby causes pecuniary disadvantage, commits economic fraud.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour, if the economic fraud causes lesser pecuniary disadvantage.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony, if
a) the economic fraud causes greater pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the economic fraud causing lesser pecuniary disadvantage is committed
ba) in a criminal association,
bb) on a commercial basis.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years, if
a) the economic fraud causes significant pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the economic fraud causing greater pecuniary disadvantage is committed in the manner specified in point ba) or bb) of paragraph (3).
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years, if
a) the economic fraud causes particularly large pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the economic fraud causing significant pecuniary disadvantage is committed in the manner specified in point ba) or bb) of paragraph (3).
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years, if
a) the economic fraud causes particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the economic fraud causing particularly large pecuniary disadvantage is committed in the manner specified in point ba) or bb) of paragraph (3).
37

## 5. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of unlawful gain, introduces data into an information system, modifies, deletes or makes inaccessible data managed therein, or influences the operation of the information system by performing other operations, and thereby causes damage, shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years, if
a) the fraud committed by using an information system causes significant damage, or
b) the fraud committed by using an information system causing greater damage is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years, if
a) the fraud committed by using an information system causes particularly large damage, or
b) the fraud committed by using an information system causing significant damage is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years, if
a) the fraud committed by using an information system causes particularly significant damage, or
b) the fraud committed by using an information system causing particularly large damage is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
(5) Whoever causes damage by using a false, falsified or unlawfully obtained electronic cash substitute payment instrument or by accepting payment with such an instrument shall be punishable under paragraphs (1)–(4).
(6) For the purposes of paragraph (5), an electronic cash substitute payment instrument issued abroad shall be afforded the same protection as a cash substitute payment instrument issued domestically.
37

## 6. §

(1) Whoever is entrusted with the management of property of another and causes pecuniary disadvantage by breaching the obligation arising therefrom, commits breach of fiduciary duty.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour, if
a) the breach of fiduciary duty causes lesser pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the breach of fiduciary duty causing pecuniary disadvantage not exceeding the petty offence value threshold is committed by a guardian or custodian in that capacity.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony, if
a) the breach of fiduciary duty causes greater pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the breach of fiduciary duty causing lesser pecuniary disadvantage is committed by a guardian or custodian in that capacity.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years, if
a) the breach of fiduciary duty causes significant pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the breach of fiduciary duty causing greater pecuniary disadvantage is committed by a guardian or custodian in that capacity.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years, if
a) the breach of fiduciary duty causes particularly large pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the breach of fiduciary duty causing significant pecuniary disadvantage is committed by a guardian or custodian in that capacity.
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years, if
a) the breach of fiduciary duty causes particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the breach of fiduciary duty causing particularly large pecuniary disadvantage is committed by a guardian or custodian in that capacity.
37

## 7. §

(1) Whoever is entrusted with the management or supervision of property of another based on law, and causes pecuniary disadvantage by negligently breaching or neglecting the obligation arising therefrom, shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years, if the negligent management causes particularly large or greater pecuniary disadvantage.
37

## 8. § (1) Whoever

a) misappropriates a found object belonging to another, or does not hand it over within eight days to the authority or to the person who lost it, or
b) misappropriates an object belonging to another that came into their possession accidentally or by mistake, or does not return it within eight days,
is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years if the misappropriation is committed in respect of an object belonging to the category of protected cultural property, an archaeological find, or a movable cultural heritage element enjoying foreign protection.
37

## 9. § *

38

## 0. §

(1) Whoever takes from another a mechanically propelled vehicle belonging to another in order to use it unlawfully, or unlawfully uses such a vehicle so taken or entrusted to them, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense is committed
a) by violence or by direct threat against life or bodily integrity,
b) in a criminal association.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offense defined in paragraph (2) point a) is committed while armed, having armed oneself, or in a criminal association.
38

## 1. §

(1) Whoever, exploiting another person's situation of need, concludes an agreement containing a disproportionately excessive consideration that is capable of exposing the obligor under the agreement, a relative of the obligor living in a common household with them, or a person maintained by the obligor on the basis of a maintenance obligation to severe or further severe deprivation through its performance, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the usury offense is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
(3) Expulsion may also be imposed on the perpetrator of a usury offense.
(4) The punishment of a person who reports the usury offense to the authority before it comes to the knowledge of the authority and discloses the circumstances of the commission may be mitigated without limitation.
38

## 2. §

* For theft, damage to property, embezzlement, fraud, fraud committed by using an information system, breach of fiduciary duty, misappropriation, or unauthorized taking of a vehicle, the perpetrator is punishable only upon private motion if the victim is their relative. This provision is not applicable if the perpetrator is also the guardian or custodian of the victim.
38

## 3. § For the purposes of this Chapter

a) thing shall also mean electricity and other economically exploitable energy, as well as a document embodying a proprietary right that in itself ensures – or in the case of a dematerialized security, to the holder of the securities account – the disposal over the proprietary value or right certified therein,
b) object of religious worship shall also mean an object used for the performance of religious ceremonies,
c) precious metal shall also mean an alloy of precious metal or material subject to metal trade licensing,
d) for the purposes of special recidivism, an offense of a similar nature is:
da) * a violent offense against property,
db) an offense against property,
dc) an offense against intellectual property.
38

## 4. § (1) Whoever

a) presents another person's intellectual creation as their own and thereby causes pecuniary disadvantage to the rightholder,
b) abusing their position, office or membership in a business organization, makes the exploitation of another person's intellectual creation or the enforcement of rights attached to the creation conditional upon being given a share of its fee or the profit or gain derived therefrom, or being presented as a rightholder,
is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) For the purposes of this Section, intellectual creation means:
a) a literary, scientific or artistic work subject to copyright protection,
b) a patentable invention,
c) a protectable plant variety,
d) a protectable utility model,
e) a protectable design,
f) a protectable topography of a microelectronic semiconductor product.
38

## 5. §

(1) Whoever violates another person's or other persons' copyright or related right or rights existing under the law on copyright, causing pecuniary disadvantage, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) A person who fails to pay the blank media levy or the reprographic levy due to the author or the holder of related rights in respect of private copying under the law on copyright is punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the violation of copyright or related rights is committed causing greater pecuniary disadvantage.
(4) If the violation of copyright or related rights
a) is committed causing significant pecuniary disadvantage, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony,
b) is committed causing particularly large pecuniary disadvantage, the punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years,
c) is committed causing particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, the punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years.
(5) A person who violates another person's or other persons' copyright or related right or rights existing under the law on copyright by reproduction or by making available on demand does not commit the offense under paragraph (1), provided that the act does not serve the purpose of income generation either directly or indirectly.
38

## 6. §

(1) Whoever circumvents an effective technological measure as defined in the law on copyright for the purpose of gain is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) A person is punishable in accordance with paragraph (1) who, for the purpose of circumventing an effective technological measure as defined in the law on copyright,
a) manufactures, produces, transfers, makes available, or places on the market a device, product, computer program, equipment or apparatus necessary for this purpose,
b) makes available to another person the economic, technical or organizational knowledge necessary for or facilitating this purpose.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the circumvention of the technological measure is committed on a commercial basis.
(4) A person who, before their activity comes to the knowledge of the authority, discloses it to the authority and hands over the manufactured or produced thing to the authority and makes it possible to establish the identity of another person participating in the manufacture or production, is not punishable for the manufacture or production of a device, product, equipment or apparatus necessary for circumventing an effective technological measure as defined in the law on copyright.
38

## 7. § Whoever for the purpose of gain

a) produces false rights management information,
b) removes or alters rights management information as defined in the law on copyright,
is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
38

## 8. §

(1) Whoever violates the right of a rightsholder arising from industrial property protection existing under law, international treaty promulgated by law or European Union legal act by
a) imitating or copying the subject matter of the protection,
b) placing on the market goods produced by imitating or copying the subject matter of the protection, or acquiring or holding such goods for the purpose of placing them on the market
and thereby causes financial detriment, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if the infringement of industrial property rights is committed on a commercial basis.
(3) If the infringement of industrial property rights is committed
a) causing significant financial detriment, the punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years,
b) causing particularly large financial detriment, the punishment shall be imprisonment from two years to eight years,
c) causing particularly significant financial detriment, the punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to ten years.
(4) For the purposes of this §
a) industrial property protection means:
aa) patent protection,
ab) plant variety protection,
ac) supplementary protection certificate,
ad) trademark protection,
ae) geographical indication protection,
af) design protection,
ag) *
ah) *
b) goods shall mean all tangible marketable movable things and services.
388/A. § * In cases under Article 1(1) of Regulation (EU) No 608/2013 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 12 June 2013 concerning customs enforcement of intellectual property rights and repealing Council Regulation (EC) No 1383/2003, the offenses defined in § 38

## 5.

and § 38

## 8.

shall be punishable only upon private motion, except where a criminal offense punishable otherwise than upon private motion is also committed in connection therewith.
388/B. § * For the purposes of this Chapter, an offense against property shall be considered a similar offense for the purposes of special recidivism.
38

## 9. § (1) Whoever

a) counterfeits or falsifies money for the purpose of placing it in circulation,
b) acquires counterfeit or falsified money for the purpose of placing it in circulation, brings it into the territory of the country, takes it out of the country, transports it through the country,
c) places counterfeit or falsified money in circulation,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from two years to eight years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if counterfeiting of money is committed
a) in respect of money of particularly large or greater value, or
b) in a criminal organization.
(3) Whoever commits preparation for counterfeiting of money shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(4) The punishment of a person who places in circulation counterfeit or falsified money of lesser or lower value lawfully acquired as genuine may be reduced without limitation.
(5) For the purposes of this §
a) money means: banknotes and coins that qualify as legal tender, or will qualify as such in the future from a specified date under law, European Union legal act or official announcement of the institution authorized to issue money, as well as banknotes and coins withdrawn from circulation if the national bank of the state that issued the withdrawn banknote or coin is obliged under law or European Union legal act to exchange it for legal tender, or has undertaken an obligation to do so,
b) securities issued in series and produced by printing shall be subject to the same assessment as banknotes, provided that their transfer is not restricted or excluded by law or by a statement entered on the security,
c) counterfeiting of money shall also include such alteration of money withdrawn from circulation as to give the appearance of money in circulation,
d) falsification of money shall also include the application or removal of a marking that serves to indicate that the money is valid only in a specific country, as well as the reduction of the precious metal content of the money.
(6) Foreign money and securities shall enjoy the same protection as domestic ones.
39

## 0. §

(1) Whoever produces, transfers, accepts, acquires, holds, brings into the territory of the country, takes out of the country, transports through the country, or places in circulation material, tool, equipment, production plan, technical description or computer program necessary for counterfeiting money shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if facilitation of counterfeiting of money is committed in a criminal organization or on a commercial basis.
39

## 1. § (1) Whoever, for the purpose of placing in circulation or use

a) counterfeits or falsifies a stamp,
b) acquires counterfeit or falsified stamp, brings it into the territory of the country, takes it out of the country, or transports it through the country,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) A person who places in circulation or uses a counterfeit, falsified or used stamp as genuine or unused shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if counterfeiting of stamps is committed
a) in respect of a stamp of particularly large or greater value, or
b) in a criminal organization.
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding one year for a misdemeanor if counterfeiting of stamps is committed in respect of a stamp of lesser or lower value.
(5) For the purposes of this §
a) stamp means:
aa) postage stamp, even if it has not yet been placed in circulation or has already been withdrawn from circulation,
ab) marking suitable for franking postal services and the imprint of a franking machine, postal overprint, inscription or marking related to payment, and international reply coupon,
ac) stamp issued by an authority for the fulfillment of a payment obligation, even if it has not yet been placed in circulation or has already been withdrawn from circulation, until such time as the state is obliged under law to redeem or exchange it,
ad) official mark serving to certify the nature or content of metal,
ae) official stamp serving to secure tax, as well as official stamp serving to certify the quality, quantity or other essential property of a product,
af) stamp and seal used by the metrological authority to certify the calibration and testing of measuring instruments and to mark the capacity of barrels,
b) placing in circulation shall also include placing in circulation for the purpose of stamp collecting,
c) falsification shall also include unauthorized alteration of a stamp serving the purpose of collecting.
(6) Foreign stamps shall enjoy the same protection as domestic ones.
39

## 2. § (1) Whoever, for the purpose of use

a) falsifies a cash-substitute payment instrument,
b) produces a counterfeit cash-substitute payment instrument, or
c) * records by technical means the data stored on an electronic cash-substitute payment instrument or the security elements associated with it,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) Whoever commits preparation for falsification of a cash-substitute payment instrument shall be punishable by confinement.
(3) Cash-substitute or electronic cash-substitute payment instruments issued abroad shall enjoy the same protection as cash-substitute payment instruments issued domestically.
39

## 3. § (1) * Whoever

a) unlawfully takes or acquires from another person, without that person's consent, one or more cash-substitute payment instruments that are not or not exclusively his or hers, or to the use of which he or she is not or not exclusively entitled,
b) transfers, acquires, brings into the territory of the country, takes out of the country, or transports through the country counterfeit or falsified cash-substitute payment instruments taken or acquired in the manner specified in point a), or the data stored on an electronic cash-substitute payment instrument or the security elements associated with it,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if abuse of cash-substitute payment instruments is committed in a criminal organization or on a commercial basis.
(3) Cash-substitute payment instruments issued abroad shall enjoy the same protection as cash-substitute payment instruments issued domestically.
39

## 4. §

(1) * Whoever produces, obtains, keeps, transfers, places into circulation, brings into the territory of the country, takes out of it, or transports through it material, tool, equipment or computer program necessary for the counterfeiting of cash substitute payment instruments or for the technical recording of data on cash substitute payment instruments, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the offense defined in paragraph (1) is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
39

## 5. §

(1) Whoever deceives another person, keeps another person in error, or conceals the true facts for the purpose of obtaining or retaining social security benefits or pecuniary or benefits in kind provided to natural persons from the subsystems of the public finances on the basis of legislation, and thereby causes damage, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) * The punishment of a person who makes restitution for the damage caused by abuse of social security, social welfare or other welfare benefits before the indictment is brought may be mitigated without limitation.
39

## 6. § (1) * Whoever

a) deceives another person, keeps another person in error, makes a false statement, or conceals the true facts in relation to an obligation to make payment into the budget or funds originating from the budget,
b) unlawfully takes advantage of a benefit in connection with an obligation to make payment into the budget, or
c) uses funds originating from the budget for a purpose other than the approved purpose,
and thereby causes pecuniary disadvantage to one or more budgets, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) *
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if
a) the budgetary fraud causes significant pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) * the budgetary fraud defined in paragraph (1) is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if
a) the budgetary fraud causes particularly large pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the budgetary fraud causing significant pecuniary disadvantage is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to ten years if
a) the budgetary fraud causes particularly significant pecuniary disadvantage, or
b) the budgetary fraud causing particularly large pecuniary disadvantage is committed in a criminal association or on a commercial basis.
(6) * A person who produces, obtains, keeps, places into circulation or trades in excise products in the absence of the conditions laid down in the Act on excise duty and in the legislation based on its authorization or without official authorization, and thereby causes pecuniary disadvantage to the budget, is punishable pursuant to paragraphs (1)–(5).
(7) Whoever fails to comply or incompletely complies with the accounting, reporting or prescribed information obligation in connection with funds originating from the budget, makes a false statement, or uses a false or forged document, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(8) * The punishment of a person who makes restitution for the pecuniary disadvantage caused by budgetary fraud defined in paragraphs (1)–(6) before the indictment is brought may be mitigated without limitation. This provision shall not apply if the offense is committed in a criminal association, in a criminal organization or as a special recidivist.
(9) For the purposes of this Section
a) budget means the budget of the subsystems of public finances – including the budget of the financial funds of social security and the separated state financial funds –, the budget managed by or on behalf of an international organization, and the budget and financial funds managed by or on behalf of the European Union. In respect of an offense committed in relation to funds originating from the budget, budget also means the budget and financial funds managed by or on behalf of a foreign state, in addition to those listed;
b) pecuniary disadvantage means the loss of revenue due to non-fulfillment of the obligation to make payment into the budget, as well as funds unlawfully claimed from the budget or used for a purpose other than the approved purpose;
c) * for the purposes of special recidivism, an offense of a similar nature is an offense damaging the budget.
39

## 7. §

The manager, member or employee authorized to control or supervise of a business organization, if he or she fails to fulfill the supervisory or control obligation and thereby enables a member or employee of the business organization to commit budgetary fraud in the course of the business organization's activities, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
39

## 8. § (1) * Whoever

a) produces, obtains, keeps, places into circulation equipment, apparatus, tool or raw material suitable for the production of excise products, as defined in the Act on excise duty and in the legislation based on its authorization, without authorization or in violation of legislation, or
b) produces, obtains or keeps tax stamps necessary for placing into circulation without authorization or in violation of legislation,
is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the offense is committed
a) on a commercial basis,
b) in relation to a significant quantity of raw material or tax stamps,
c) in relation to tax stamps of significant or greater value.
(3) For the purposes of point b) of paragraph (2)
a) the raw material is of significant quantity if it exceeds
aa) 20,000 liters in the case of mineral oil products without tax rate that can be used as motor gasoline or gas oil, as motor gasoline or gas oil additive, or as diluent,
ab) 45,000 kilograms in the case of liquid hydrocarbons without tax rate,
ac) 100,000 nm3 in the case of gaseous hydrocarbons without tax rate,
ad) 10,000 liters in the case of sugar wash,
ae) 25,000 liters in the case of wash made from agricultural products containing sugar or starch,
af) * 5 kilograms in the case of dried tobacco, fermented tobacco or cut tobacco,
b) the tax stamp is of significant quantity if it exceeds 5,000 pieces.
39

## 9. §

* (1) Whoever conceals or disguises the origin of property derived from a punishable act, the right over the property, the location of the property, or changes thereto, commits money laundering.
(2) Whoever, for the purpose of concealing or disguising the origin of property derived from a punishable act, the right over the property, the location of the property, or changes thereto, receives the property from another, hides it, converts it, transfers it, participates in its alienation, uses it, conducts financial activity in connection with it, uses financial services, or disposes of it, also commits money laundering.
(3) Whoever, by receiving property derived from a punishable act from another, hiding it, converting it, transferring it, participating in its alienation, using it, conducting financial activity in connection with it, using financial services, or disposing of it,
a) participates in frustrating confiscation of property or asset recovery against another, or
b) seeks to frustrate confiscation of property or asset recovery against another,
also commits money laundering.
(4) Whoever
a) acquires property derived from a punishable act committed by another, acquires the right of disposal over it, or
b) preserves, hides, manages, uses, utilizes, converts, transfers it, or participates in its alienation,
also commits money laundering.
(5) The punishment for a felony is imprisonment not exceeding five years if the money laundering is committed for a value not exceeding significant value.
(6) The punishment is imprisonment from two to eight years if the money laundering is committed
a) for a particularly large value, or
b) for a significant value
ba) as a business,
bb) as a service provider, its officer or employee defined in the law on the prevention and combating of money laundering and terrorist financing, in connection with the service provider's activity, or
bc) as an official person.
(7) The punishment is imprisonment from five to ten years if the money laundering is committed
a) for a particularly significant value, or
b) for a particularly large value
ba) as a business,
bb) as a service provider, its officer or employee defined in the law on the prevention and combating of money laundering and terrorist financing, in connection with the service provider's activity, or
bc) as an official person.
(8) Whoever commits preparation for money laundering is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(9) The instigator or accessory is not punishable if he commits the offense defined in subsection (3) or (4) in relation to property derived from a punishable act committed by him.
40

## 0. §

* (1) Whoever hides, converts, transfers, participates in the alienation of, uses property derived from a punishable act committed by another, conducts financial activity in connection with it, uses financial services or disposes of it, and through negligence does not know about the origin of the property, is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) The punishment for a misdemeanor is imprisonment not exceeding three years if the offense defined in subsection (1) is committed
a) for a particularly large or greater value,
b) as a service provider, its officer or employee defined in the law on the prevention and combating of money laundering and terrorist financing, in connection with the service provider's activity, or
c) as an official person.
(3) Whoever voluntarily reports to the authorities and discloses the circumstances of the commission is not punishable for money laundering defined in subsections (1) and (2), provided that the offense has not yet been discovered or has only been partially discovered.
40

## 1. §

Whoever fails to fulfill the reporting obligation prescribed by law in connection with the prevention and combating of money laundering and terrorist financing is punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
40

## 2. § (1) *

(2) * For the purposes of §§ 399–40

## 0. §

financial activity and the use of financial services shall mean financial service or ancillary financial service, investment service or service ancillary to investment service, commodity exchange service, investment fund management, venture capital fund management, stock exchange, central securities depository or central counterparty, or insurance, reinsurance or independent insurance intermediary, or voluntary mutual insurance fund, private pension fund or occupational pension service activity, or the use thereof.
40

## 3. §

(1) Whoever violates the voucher system prescribed in the law on accounting or in legislation based on its authorization, or breaches his bookkeeping or financial statement preparation obligation, and thereby
a) * causes an error materially affecting the reliable and true view, or
b) frustrates the review or control of his financial position for the given business year,
is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Individual entrepreneurs and other economic operators not subject to the law on accounting who breach their registration and voucher obligations prescribed by law and thereby frustrate the review or control of their financial position are also punishable according to subsection (1).
(3) * The punishment in the case of subsection (1) is imprisonment from two to eight years if the offense is committed in the context of a financial institution, investment firm, commodity exchange service provider, investment fund manager, venture capital fund manager, stock exchange, central securities depository or central counterparty organization, insurer, reinsurer or independent insurance intermediary, voluntary mutual insurance fund, private pension fund or occupational pension institution, or regulated real estate investment company.
(4) * For the purposes of this §, an error materially affects the reliable and true view if the combined value of errors and error effects – increasing or decreasing profit or equity – detected in relation to a given business year, independent of sign, exceeds twenty percent of the net sales revenue and also twenty percent of the balance sheet total shown in the financial statement for the business year in which the error was committed. In all cases, an error materially affects the reliable and true view if the combined value of errors and error effects – increasing or decreasing profit or equity – detected in relation to a given business year, independent of sign, exceeds five hundred million forints.
40

## 4. §

(1) Anyone who, in the case of an economic organisation subject to the Act on Bankruptcy Proceedings and Liquidation Proceedings, in a situation threatening insolvency,
a) by hiding, concealing, damaging, destroying, rendering unusable the assets or a part thereof,
b) by concluding a simulated transaction or acknowledging a dubious claim, or
c) in another manner contrary to the requirements of reasonable management
actually or apparently reduces the assets of the economic organisation, and thereby frustrates the satisfaction of the creditor or creditors in part or in whole, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) Punishable according to paragraph (1) is anyone who, in respect of an economic organisation subject to the Act on Bankruptcy Proceedings and Liquidation Proceedings,
a) causes its insolvency or the appearance thereof by any of the conducts specified in paragraph (1), and thereby, or
b) in the case of its insolvency, by any of the conducts specified in paragraph (1),
frustrates the satisfaction of the creditor or creditors in part or in whole.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if
a) * the bankruptcy offence is committed in respect of an economic organisation subject to bankruptcy proceedings or liquidation proceedings of strategically outstanding importance, or
b) the extent of the actual or apparent reduction of assets is particularly significant.
(4) Anyone who, following the ordering of liquidation, gives preference to any of its creditors in violation of the order of satisfaction specified in the Act on Bankruptcy Proceedings and Liquidation Proceedings, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment for up to two years.
(5) The offence specified in paragraphs (1)-(3) is punishable if
a) the bankruptcy proceedings have been initiated,
b) the liquidation, compulsory deletion or compulsory final settlement proceedings have been ordered, or
c) the initiation of liquidation proceedings has not taken place contrary to the mandatory provision of law.
(6) The bankruptcy offence may be committed as a perpetrator by anyone who is entitled to dispose of the assets of the debtor economic organisation or a part thereof, or has the possibility to do so, even if the legal transaction serving as the basis for the disposal of the assets is invalid.
404/A. § * A liquidator who, during the liquidation proceedings, obtains credible knowledge of the violation of the accounting rules (40

## 3. §)

or bankruptcy offence (40

## 4. §)

and does not report this to the authority as soon as possible, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment for up to one year.
40

## 5. §

(1) Anyone who withdraws in part or in whole the assets serving as security for a claim existing on the basis of a written contract, and thereby frustrates the settlement of the debt in part or in whole, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment for up to one year.
(2) Anyone who commits the act specified in paragraph (1) in respect of assets serving as security for a debt arising from economic activity shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the offence specified in paragraph (2) is committed for a particularly large value or exceeding that.
(4) * The perpetrator shall not be punishable for withdrawal of security for a debt if he or she settles the debt before the bringing of charges.
40

## 6. §

Anyone who brings goods into the territory of the country or takes them out from there without the permit necessary for their import or export shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.
40

## 7. §

An executive officer or member of a public limited company, a limited liability company, a cooperative, a European public limited company or a European cooperative who withdraws in part or in whole the company's own capital shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.
40

## 8. §

* (1) * Anyone who, without a permit prescribed by law or in a directly applicable legal act of the European Union, carries out
a) financial services or ancillary financial services,
b) investment services or services ancillary to investment services, commodity exchange services, investment fund management, venture capital fund management, stock exchange, central securities depository or central counterparty,
c) insurance, reinsurance,
d) voluntary mutual insurance fund, private pension fund, occupational pension provision,
e) crowdfunding services
activity, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.
(1a) * Punishable according to paragraph (1) is anyone who carries out pan-European personal pension product service or distribution activity without registration in the central register maintained by the European Insurance and Occupational Pensions Authority.
(2) Anyone who, in connection with any of the activities specified in paragraph (1) points a)-c),
a) carries out intermediary activity without a permit prescribed by law,
b) * carries out intermediary activity or electronic money distribution activity without registration or notification prescribed by law in such a manner that he or she failed to verify that it has taken place,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment for up to two years.
40

## 9. §

(1) A person in a leading position of an economic organisation who participates in ensuring that
a) the economic organisation cannot be found at its registered office, place of business or branch,
b) a person whose place of residence or place of stay is unknown or is deemed unknown is entered in a public register as a person authorised to represent the economic organisation, or
c) a person or economic organisation whose place of residence or place of stay is unknown or is deemed unknown, or which cannot be found at its registered office, place of business or branch, or who or which is not the beneficial owner, is entered in a public register as the owner of the economic organisation,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.
(2) Anyone who fails to notify data, right or fact to be entered in a public register, related to economic activity, or fails to notify a change in such data, right or fact, if the notification obligation is prescribed by law, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment for up to two years.
(3) For the purposes of this §, a beneficial owner means the person or economic organisation who or which holds directly or indirectly at least ten per cent of the voting rights or ownership share in the economic organisation or has controlling influence as defined in the Civil Code.
41

## 0. § * (1) Whoever

a) using insider information, concludes a transaction in their own name or in the name of another person in respect of a financial instrument affected by the insider information, gives an instruction to conclude a transaction, cancels or modifies an instruction, places, cancels or modifies an order,
b) in view of the insider information in their possession, calls upon or induces another person or persons to conclude a transaction in respect of a financial instrument affected by the insider information, to give, cancel or modify an instruction to conclude a transaction, or to place, cancel or modify an order,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The penalty shall be imprisonment not exceeding five years if
a) the criminal offence is committed as an official person,
b) the criminal offence is committed as an officer or employee of a financial institution, investment firm, commodity exchange service provider, investment fund manager, venture capital fund manager, organisation carrying out stock exchange, central securities depository or central counterparty activities, insurer, reinsurer or independent insurance intermediary, voluntary mutual insurance fund, private pension fund, occupational pension institution, or regulated real estate investment company,
c) * the criminal offence is committed as an officer or employee of a publicly offered securities issuer who has access to the insider information by virtue of their duties,
d) * the absolute value of the transaction or transactions, instruction or instructions, order or orders affected by the criminal offence is particularly large or exceeds that value.
410/A. § * Whoever, in order to obtain an unlawful advantage or cause unlawful disadvantage, discloses insider information to an unauthorised person or persons, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
41

## 1. § * (1) Whoever

a) * concludes a transaction, gives, cancels or modifies an instruction, places, cancels or modifies an order in the course of dealing on own account, or engages in any other conduct which gives false or misleading signals as to the supply, demand, price or value of a given financial instrument or related spot commodity contract, or secures it at an artificial or abnormal level,
b) seeks to influence the price or value of a financial instrument or related spot commodity contract by concluding a fictitious transaction in respect of a financial instrument or by other deceptive conduct,
c) seeks to influence the calculation of a benchmark by communicating or transmitting false or misleading information or by applying a deceptive method,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Whoever, for the purpose of obtaining profit, communicates or spreads information before the general public which gives false or misleading signals as to the supply, demand, price or value of a given financial instrument or related spot commodity contract, or secures it at an artificial or abnormal level, shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) Whoever, by communicating or spreading false data, or by concealing data, concerning the financial position of an economic organisation or a person in a leading position in connection with this activity, or concerning a financial instrument in respect of the economic organisation, induces others to make a capital investment or increase an investment, or to sell a capital investment or reduce an investment, shall also be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(4) * Whoever commits the criminal offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3)
a) in a criminal organisation,
b) as an official person,
c) as an officer or employee of a financial institution, investment firm, commodity exchange service provider, investment fund manager, venture capital fund manager, organisation carrying out stock exchange, central securities depository or central counterparty activities, insurer, reinsurer or independent insurance intermediary, voluntary mutual insurance fund, private pension fund, occupational pension institution, or regulated real estate investment company, or
d) as an officer or employee of a publicly offered securities issuer
shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding five years.
(5) Paragraphs (1)–(4) shall also apply to the transactions and instruments defined in Article 2(2)(a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 596/2014 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 16 April 2014 on market abuse (market abuse regulation) and repealing Directive 2003/6/EC of the European Parliament and of the Council and Commission Directives 2003/124/EC, 2003/125/EC and 2004/72/EC (hereinafter: Regulation 596/2014/EU) if the transaction, instruction, order or conduct has an effect on the price, value or rate of the financial instrument or spot commodity contract.
41

## 2. §

Whoever organises a game based on the collection and distribution of other people's money in a predetermined form and in a manner that also contains a risk factor, in which the participants joining in a chain-like manner make a payment or provide another service directly, or through the organiser, to the participants preceding them in the chain, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
41

## 3. §

(1) A person obliged to maintain banking, securities, pension fund, insurance or occupational pension secrecy who, for the purpose of obtaining an unlawful advantage or causing financial disadvantage to another person, makes data constituting banking, securities, pension fund, insurance or occupational pension secrecy accessible to an unauthorised person, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) A person does not commit the violation of business secrecy who
a) fulfils their obligation defined in the Act on the publicity of data of public interest and data public in the public interest, or
b) fulfils or initiates their reporting obligation prescribed by law in connection with the prevention and combating of money laundering and terrorist financing, insider dealing, market manipulation and the fight against terrorism, even if the report made by them in good faith was unfounded.
41

## 4. §

(1) * For the purposes of Sections 410–41

## 1. §

financial instrument means a financial instrument as defined in the Act on investment firms and commodity exchange service providers and on the rules of the activities they may pursue, provided that it complies with the provisions of Article 2(1) of Regulation 596/2014/EU, excluding
a) the cases defined in Article 5(1)–(3) of Regulation 596/2014/EU;
b) the cases defined in Article 5(4) and (5) of Regulation 596/2014/EU;
c) the cases defined in Article 6(1)–(4) of Regulation 596/2014/EU.
(2) For the purposes of Section 409 and Section 41

## 1. § person in a leading position means

a) an executive officer of the economic organisation, a member of its supervisory board,
b) a person appointed to manage the Hungarian branch of a foreign-based undertaking or the Hungarian establishment of a foreign-based European Economic Interest Grouping,
c) any person whom the articles of association, deed of foundation or articles of partnership of the economic organisation defines as such.
41


This excerpt sits under: The current state of the legislation as of today (14.02.2024).

## 5. §

(1) Whoever places a product of poor quality on the market as a product of good quality shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the offence is committed
a) in respect of a product of poor quality in significant quantity or value,
b) in a criminal organisation, or
c) as a business
(3) Whoever commits preparatory acts for placing a product of poor quality on the market shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(4) Whoever commits the offence by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(5) The perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraph (4) shall not be punishable if, as soon as he becomes aware of the poor quality of the product, he does everything to ensure that the product of poor quality is returned to his possession.
(6) * For the purposes of this Section, a product shall be of poor quality
a) if it does not meet the safety or quality requirements laid down in legislation or in a directly applicable legal act of general application of the European Union, or
b) in the absence of the provision referred to in point (a), if
ba) the unsuitability for human consumption of a product manufactured for human consumption has been concealed,
bb) the best-before date, use-by date or expiry date of the product has been unlawfully extended,
bc) the product has been manufactured using a non-permitted ingredient,
bd) the product has been manufactured from materials or products unfit for human consumption for the purpose of human consumption, or
be) the product cannot be used for its intended purpose or its usability has been significantly reduced.
41

## 6. §

(1) Whoever certifies false information about the conformity of a product in significant quantity or value in a certificate of conformity or declaration of conformity to be issued in the case of a conformity assessment obligation laid down in legislation or in a directly applicable legal act of the European Union, or by using a conformity marking, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one year to five years if the offence is committed
a) in a criminal organisation, or
b) as a business
(3) Whoever commits the offence by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
41

## 7. §

(1) Whoever provides information capable of misleading about the existence of a special price reduction or price advantage, or about the chance of winning at an organised product presentation shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(2) Whoever, for the purpose of selling goods, states a false fact or a true fact in a manner capable of misleading, or provides information capable of misleading, before a large public or in respect of an essential characteristic of goods in significant quantity or value, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the offence defined in paragraph (2) is committed in connection with the characteristics of the goods relating to their effect on health or the environment, their dangerousness, their risks or their safety.
(4) For the purposes of this Section
a) an essential characteristic of goods means:
aa) the composition, technical characteristics and suitability of the goods for the given purpose,
ab) * the origin, place of origin and method of production of the goods,
ac) the testing, verification or result thereof of the goods;
b) organised product presentation means: retail activity carried out during a trip or event organised for the purpose of marketing a product.
41

## 8. §

Whoever unlawfully obtains, uses, makes accessible to another person or discloses a trade secret for the purpose of obtaining an unlawful advantage or causing financial detriment to another shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
41

## 9. §

(1) Whoever, without the consent of a competitor, manufactures goods with a distinctive appearance, packaging, marking or designation by which the competitor or its goods with distinctive characteristics can be identified, or obtains, holds or places on the market such goods for the purpose of placing them on the market, if no other offence is committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony if the offence is committed in respect of imitated goods in significant quantity or value.
42

## 0. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of influencing the outcome of a public procurement procedure or an open or restricted tender announced for a concession activity, concludes an agreement or engages in other concerted conduct aimed at fixing prices, fees or other contractual conditions or dividing the market, and thereby restricts competition, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) Whoever, for the purpose of influencing the outcome of a public procurement procedure or an open or restricted tender announced for a concession activity, participates in the adoption of a decision by an association of undertakings, a public body, an association or other similar organisation which restricts competition, shall be punishable in accordance with paragraph (1).
(3) The punishment shall be imprisonment not exceeding two years for a misdemeanour if the offence defined in paragraph (1) or (2) is committed in respect of a public procurement value not exceeding a significant value.
(4) * The perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall not be punishable if he reports the act to the authority before it comes to the attention of the authority acting in criminal matters and discloses the circumstances of the commission.
(5) The perpetrator of the offence defined in paragraphs (1)–(3) shall not be punishable if, at the time of committing the act, he is a leading officer, member, member of the supervisory board, employee or agent thereof of an undertaking which – before the authority acting in competition supervision matters has initiated an investigation in the case – has submitted an application giving grounds for the waiver of a fine under the law on the prohibition of restriction of competition covering the act, and discloses the circumstances of the commission.
(6) The punishment may be reduced without limitation, and in cases deserving special consideration may be waived, in respect of a person who, at the time of committing the offence, is a leading officer, member, member of the supervisory board, employee or agent thereof of an undertaking which has submitted to the authority acting in competition supervision matters an application giving grounds for the waiver or reduction of a fine under the law on the prohibition of restriction of competition covering the act, and discloses the circumstances of the commission.
42

## 1. § For the purposes of this Chapter

a) product means: any tangible, marketable movable thing and natural forces that can be exploited in the manner of a thing,
b) goods means: the product, immovable property, service and right of pecuniary value.
42

## 2. §

(1) Anyone who, for the purpose of unlawfully obtaining personal data, private secrets, economic secrets or business secrets,
a) * secretly searches another person's dwelling, other premises belonging to it, or the enclosed area belonging to them,
b) * secretly observes or records events occurring in another person's dwelling, other premises belonging to it, or the enclosed area belonging to them, by using technical means,
c) * secretly opens or obtains another person's postal consignment or other closed consignment, and records its content with technical means,
d) * secretly intercepts the content of communication conducted via electronic communications network or device, or on an information system, and records what is perceived with technical means,
e) * secretly intercepts data processed in an information system, and records what is perceived with technical means,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(1a) * Anyone who, for the purpose of unlawfully obtaining personal data, private secrets, economic secrets or business secrets,
a) * secretly searches premises or areas other than public places or places open to the public, vehicles – with the exception of public transport vehicles – or objects in another person's use,
b) secretly observes or records events occurring in premises or areas other than public places or places open to the public, or in vehicles – with the exception of public transport vehicles – by using technical means,
shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(2) * Anyone who collects information beyond that specified in paragraph (1) for the purpose of establishing the identity or activity of an undercover investigator or a person secretly cooperating with an authority authorized to conduct covert intelligence gathering or to use covert means shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(3) Anyone who transmits or uses personal data, private secrets, economic secrets or business secrets obtained in the manner specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable according to paragraph (1).
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the unlawful data acquisition specified in paragraphs (1)–(3) is committed
a) by simulating official proceedings,
b) as a business,
c) in a criminal organization, or
d) causing significant harm to interests.
422/A. § * (1) Anyone who, by unauthorized use of an unmanned aircraft, observes another person's dwelling, other premises, or the enclosed area belonging to them, and records the events occurring there, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by confinement, if no other criminal offense is committed.
(2) Anyone who makes audio or video recordings made during the observation specified in paragraph (1) accessible to the general public shall be punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year, if no other criminal offense is committed.
(3) The criminal offenses specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) shall be punishable only upon private motion.
42

## 3. §

* (1) Anyone who unlawfully enters an information system by breaching or circumventing the technical measures ensuring the protection of the information system, or remains in it beyond or in breach of their access authorization, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) Anyone who
a) unlawfully or in breach of the limits of their authorization obstructs the operation of an information system, or
b) unlawfully or in breach of the limits of their authorization alters, deletes or makes inaccessible data in an information system,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(3) * The punishment shall be imprisonment from one to five years for a felony if the criminal offense specified in paragraph (2) affects a significant number of information systems or causes significant harm to interests.
(4) The punishment shall be imprisonment from two to eight years if the criminal offense is committed against a public utility.
(5) For the purposes of this Section, data means: any representation of facts, information or concepts stored, processed, handled or transmitted in an information system in a form suitable for processing by an information system, including any program that enables the performance of a function by an information system.
42

## 4. §

(1) * Anyone who, for the purpose of committing the criminal offense specified in 37

## 5. §

, in point d) of paragraph (1) of Section 42

## 2. §

or in Section 42

## 3. §

, prepares, transfers, makes accessible, obtains or places into circulation
a) a password or computer program necessary for or facilitating this, or
b) makes available to another person their economic, technical or organizational knowledge relating to the preparation of a password or computer program,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) * The perpetrator of the criminal offense specified in point a) of paragraph (1) shall not be punishable if – before the preparation of the password or computer program necessary for or facilitating the commission of the criminal offense came to the knowledge of the authority acting in criminal matters – they disclose their activity to the authority, hand over the prepared item to the authority, and enable the establishment of the identity of other persons participating in the preparation.
(3) For the purposes of this Section, password means: any identifier consisting of numbers, letters, characters, biometric data or a combination thereof, enabling access to an information system or a part thereof.
42

## 5. §

(1) A person liable for military service who fails to fulfill their military call-up obligation shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) Anyone who commits the criminal offense by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
42

## 6. §

A person liable for military service who, for the purpose of evading military service,
a) fails to fulfill their obligation to appear or to be called up, or
b) mutilates their body, harms their health, or engages in deceptive conduct,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to ten years.
42

## 7. §

A person liable for military service who refuses to perform their military service shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.
42

## 8. §

(1) Anyone who commits an act aimed at preventing a person liable for military service from fulfilling their call-up obligation shall be punishable for a felony according to Section 42

## 5. §
