(2) * Anyone who commits an act aimed at evading a person liable for military service from military service
a) in the manner specified in point a) of Section 42

## 6. §

, or

b) in the manner specified in point b) of Section 42

## 6. §

shall be punishable according to the provisions specified therein.
42

## 9. §

(1) * Anyone who fails to fulfill their civil defense obligation shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from one to five years if the criminal offense causes serious danger.
(3) Anyone who commits the criminal offense specified in paragraph (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
43

## 0. §

A person obligated to perform defense work who seriously breaches this obligation by absence or in another manner shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
43

## 1. §

* Anyone who seriously breaches or evades their defense and security obligation consisting of economic and material services shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
43

## 2. §

The punishment may be mitigated without limitation if the perpetrator of a criminal offense specified in this Chapter voluntarily fulfills the obligation they failed to perform.
43

## 3. § (1) * The provisions of this Chapter shall be applied in a state of war

(1a) * Point b) of Section 42

## 6. §

and point b) of paragraph (2) of Section 42

## 8. §

shall also be applied during the period following the Government's initiation of a state of war.
(2) * Breach of civil defense obligation may be committed, in addition to what is specified in paragraph (1), in a state of emergency and in a state of danger, and breach of service obligation may be committed in a state of danger.
(3) * Breach of defense work obligation may only be committed in a state of war.
43

## 4. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of permanently evading the performance of their military service, arbitrarily leaves their place of service or stays away from it, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the desertion is committed
a) while armed,
b) in a group,
c) during the performance of important service or by exploiting the service,
d) by using violence against a person, or
e) abroad.
(3) * Whoever deserts abroad in the manner specified in points a)–c) of paragraph (2), or whoever commits the desertion in time of war, is punishable by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.
(4) Whoever commits preparation for desertion as specified in paragraph (2) is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year, or in the case specified in paragraph (3) for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(5) The punishment of the perpetrator of desertion may be reduced without limitation if they voluntarily report to the authorities within thirty days. The day of desertion is not included in the time limit.
43

## 5. §

(1) Whoever arbitrarily leaves their place of service or stays away from it, and their absence exceeds two days, is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(2) If the duration of the unauthorised absence exceeds nine days, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.
(3) * If the unauthorised absence is committed in time of war, during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping in a foreign theatre of operations, the punishment is
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from two years to eight years.
43

## 6. §

(1) Whoever, for the purpose of permanently evading the performance of their military service, mutilates their body, damages their health, or engages in deceptive conduct, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) Whoever commits the offence specified in paragraph (1) in order to temporarily evade the performance of their military service is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(3) If the duration of the temporary evasion exceeds six days, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(4) * If the offence is committed in time of war, the punishment is
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from five years to fifteen years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony,
c) in the case specified in paragraph (3), imprisonment from two years to eight years.
43

## 7. §

(1) Whoever expressly refuses to perform military service is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(1a) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the refusal of service is committed during a state of defence emergency.
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the refusal of service is committed in time of war.
43

## 8. §

(1) Whoever, in guard, watch or other standby duty – in violation of the provisions of the service order applicable to the given service – falls asleep, or during the service consumes alcoholic beverages, or a narcotic drug or an intoxicating substance or agent not classified as a narcotic drug, leaves their designated place, or otherwise seriously violates the provisions relating to the performance of the service, is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(2) If the offence entails a risk of significant detriment to the service, the punishment is for a felony
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment not exceeding three years,
b) * in time of war, imprisonment from one year to five years.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years for a felony if the offence is committed in a combat situation or during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping in a foreign theatre of operations, and results in particularly great detriment.
(4) Whoever commits the offence by negligence is punishable for a misdemeanour in the case specified in paragraph (2), according to the distinction made therein, by confinement or imprisonment not exceeding one year, respectively, and in the case of paragraph (3) by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
43

## 9. §

(1) Whoever evades an important service task by deception or absence, or renders themselves incapable of performing it, commits a misdemeanour and is punishable by imprisonment not exceeding one year.
(2) If the offence specified in paragraph (1) entails a risk of significant detriment to the service or is committed during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping in a foreign theatre of operations, the punishment is imprisonment not exceeding three years for a felony.
(3) * If the offence specified in paragraph (2) is committed in time of war, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years.
44

## 0. §

(1) Whoever fails to make a report in due time in an important service matter, or makes a false report, if the offence entails a risk of significant detriment to the service, is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if the offence specified in paragraph (1) is committed in time of war, during humanitarian activities or peacekeeping in a foreign theatre of operations.
44

## 1. §

(1) Whoever, in order to cause unlawful detriment or to obtain an unlawful advantage, abuses their service authority or position, if a more serious offence is not committed, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if the offence results in significant detriment.
44

## 2. §

(1) Whoever participates in collective, open defiance directed against the service order and discipline, which significantly disrupts the performance of service tasks, is punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(2) The initiator, organiser or leader of the mutiny, or
b) a participant thereof, if they use violence against a superior or against a person acting against the mutiny,
is punishable by imprisonment from two years to eight years.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the mutiny results in particularly serious consequences.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to twenty years or life imprisonment if the mutiny causes death.
(5) * The punishment is
a) in the case specified in paragraph (1), imprisonment from two years to eight years,
b) in the case specified in paragraph (2), imprisonment from five years to fifteen years,
if the mutiny is committed in time of war.
(6) If the mutiny specified in paragraphs (1)–(2) is committed in a combat situation, the punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.
(7) * Whoever commits preparation for mutiny is punishable for a felony by imprisonment not exceeding three years, or in time of war or in a combat situation by imprisonment from one year to five years.
(8) In the case specified in paragraph (1), the punishment of a person who abandons the mutiny before it results in more serious consequences or after being called upon to do so may be reduced without limitation.
44

## 3. §

(1) Whoever does not prevent, to the extent possible, a mutiny or its preparation that has come to their knowledge, or does not report it without delay, is punishable for a misdemeanour by imprisonment not exceeding two years.
(2) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years for a felony if the offence specified in paragraph (1) is committed in time of war or in a combat situation.
44

## 4. § (1) Anyone who fails to obey an order shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment

(2) If disobedience to an order is committed in a group, the punishment is imprisonment for up to two years.
(3) The punishment for a felony is imprisonment for up to three years if the disobedience to an order
a) occurs in the presence of other subordinates or otherwise publicly, whether by express refusal to obey the order or in another offensive manner,
b) entails the risk of significant detriment to the service or to discipline.
(4) * The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense defined in paragraph (3) is committed in time of war.
(5) Anyone who fails to obey a combat order in war, or an order concerning the use of weapons during humanitarian activity or peacekeeping carried out in a foreign theater of operations, shall be punishable by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.
44

## 5. § (1) Anyone who

a) against a superior,
b) against a senior, a guard or other service agent
uses violence, threatens with it, or offers physical resistance during or because of the performance of their service, shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment for up to three years.
(2) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if
a) the offense is committed armed, in arms or in a group,
b) the offense is also disobedience to an order,
c) the offense entails serious bodily injury or the risk of significant detriment to the service or to discipline.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from two years to eight years if the offense causes permanent disability, serious deterioration of health or danger to life.
(4) The punishment is imprisonment from five years to fifteen years if the offense causes the death of the victim.
(5) The punishment is imprisonment from ten years to twenty years or life imprisonment if
a) the offense also constitutes intentional homicide, or
b) the offense is committed in a combat situation.
(6) * The punishment is
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1), from one year to five years,
b) in the case defined in paragraph (2), from two years to eight years,
c) in the case defined in paragraph (3), from five years to fifteen years
imprisonment if the offense is committed in time of war.
44

## 6. §

Anyone who commits the offense defined in 44

## 5. §

against a person who has come to the defense of a superior or service agent or has been assigned to do so shall be punishable accordingly.
44

## 7. § (1) Anyone who

a) the authority of a superior,
b) the authority of a senior, guard or other service agent performing their service
offends before others or in a conspicuously rude manner, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment for up to one year.
(2) The punishment for a felony is imprisonment for up to three years if the offense is committed before several soldiers or otherwise publicly.
44

## 8. §

(1) Anyone who incites discontent among soldiers towards a superior, an order, the service order or discipline shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment for up to one year.
(2) The punishment for a felony is imprisonment for up to three years if the incitement results in significant detriment to the service or to discipline.
44

## 9. §

(1) Anyone who offends their subordinate in their human dignity before others or in a conspicuously rude manner shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment for up to one year.
(2) The punishment for a felony is imprisonment for up to three years if the offense is committed
a) from base motives,
b) causing severe physical or mental suffering,
c) before several soldiers,
d) to the detriment of several subordinates.
(3) The punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years if the offense causes serious bodily injury or significant detriment to the service.
45

## 0. § Anyone who, abusing their superior authority,

a) subjects their subordinate to disciplinary punishment,
b) restricts them in the exercise of their right to complain,
c) shortchanges them in their entitlements or burdens them financially,
d) uses them for private purposes,
e) treats them more favorably or less favorably compared to others,
shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment for up to one year.
45

## 1. §

(1) Anyone who violates their superior duty by failing to take the necessary measures for the material provision of their subordinate, for protecting them from imminent danger or for their rescue, if a more serious offense is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment for up to one year.
(2) If the offense entails significant detriment to the service or to discipline, the punishment for a felony is
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1), from one year to five years,
b) * in time of war, from two years to eight years
imprisonment.
(3) Anyone who commits the offense defined in paragraph (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment for up to one year or up to three years, respectively.
45

## 2. § (1) Anyone who, in violation of their superior duty,

a) fails to take the necessary measures to prevent or hold accountable a subordinate's breach of duty or offense,
b) fails to take the necessary measures to overcome a disturbance threatening the service order, discipline or public safety,
if a crime against humanity or war crime is not committed, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment for up to one year.
(2) If the offense entails significant detriment to the service, to discipline or to public safety, the punishment for a felony is
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1), from one year to five years,
b) * in time of war, from two years to eight years
imprisonment.
(3) Anyone who commits the offense defined in paragraph (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment for up to one year or up to three years, respectively.
45

## 3. §

(1) Anyone who violates their superior duty by failing to supervise their subordinate in the performance of their service, and this entails significant detriment to the service or to discipline, shall be punishable for a misdemeanor by imprisonment for up to one year.
(2) If the offense entails particularly great detriment to the service or to discipline, the punishment for a felony is
a) in the case defined in paragraph (1), from one year to five years,
b) * in time of war, from two years to eight years
imprisonment.
(3) Anyone who commits the offense defined in paragraph (2) by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment for up to one year or up to three years, respectively.
45

## 4. §

(1) Anyone who directly endangers the readiness level of the unit by, in violation of their service duty,
a) failing to ensure the necessary armament, combat equipment or other war material or to protect its stock,
b) destroying, rendering unusable or otherwise diverting from its purpose important armament, combat equipment or other important war material,
commits a felony and shall be punishable by imprisonment for up to three years, in time of war from two years to eight years.
(2) * If the offense entails particularly great detriment to the service, the punishment is imprisonment from one year to five years, in time of war imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.
(3) Anyone who commits the offense by negligence shall be punishable for a misdemeanor in the case of paragraph (1) – according to the distinction made therein – by imprisonment for up to one year or up to three years, respectively, in the case of paragraph (2) – according to the distinction made therein – for up to two years or from one year to five years, respectively.
45

## 5. § Anyone who, in a combat situation, in violation of their command duty,

a) surrenders the subordinate soldiers to the enemy or allows them to be captured,
b) destroys without compelling necessity the important combat position, installation, combat equipment or other war material entrusted to them, or hands it over to the enemy in usable condition,
c) fails to offer the resistance against the enemy of which they are capable,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five years to fifteen years.
45

## 6. § Anyone who evades the performance of their combat duty

a) by unauthorized abandonment of their post, by hiding or by fleeing,
b) by deliberately causing incapacity for combat or by deceptive conduct,
c) by abandoning, damaging or failing to use their weapon,
d) by voluntary surrender to the enemy, or
e) by other serious breach of their service duty,
shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from five to fifteen years.
45

## 7. §

(1) * Anyone who in time of war incites discontent among soldiers or creates defeatism shall be punishable for a felony by imprisonment from one to five years.
(2) The punishment shall be imprisonment from five to fifteen years if the criminal offense
a) triggers discontent or other breach of duty among soldiers in a combat situation, or
b) results in other significant detriment to the service.
45

## 8. § For the purposes of this chapter

a) military service means the service performed by the persons specified in Section 12

## 7. §

(1), paragraph (1),
b) combat situation means the conduct of actual combat activity.
45

## 9. § (1) For the purposes of this Act

1. * criminal organization: a group consisting of at least three persons, organized hierarchically for a longer period of time, operating conspiratorially, the purpose of which is to commit intentional criminal offenses punishable by imprisonment of five years or more;
2. criminal association is established when two or more persons commit criminal offenses in an organized manner, or agree to do so, and attempt to commit at least one criminal offense, but a criminal organization is not formed;
3. a criminal offense is committed by a group if at least three persons participate in its commission;
4. violent conduct also includes aggressive physical action against another person, even if it is not capable of causing bodily injury;
5. commits the criminal offense while armed, who
a) * has in their possession a functional firearm,
b) explosives,
c) blasting agents,
d) a device designed for the use of explosives or blasting agents
or commits the criminal offense by threatening with an imitation of those specified in points a)–d);
6. commits the criminal offense while equipped with weapons, who has in their possession an instrument capable of taking life in order to overcome or prevent resistance;
7. threat: in the absence of a different provision, the prospect of serious harm, which is capable of causing serious fear in the person threatened;
8. * business organization: in addition to the business organization under the code of civil procedure, also an organization whose civil law relations in connection with its business activity are subject to the provisions applicable to business organizations under the code of civil procedure;
9. * authority includes the court and the prosecution service;
10. war:
a) the situations defined in the common Articles 2 and 3 of the Geneva Conventions of 12 August 1949 for the protection of war victims, and in paragraph 4 of Article 1 of Additional Protocol I to these Conventions,
b) the situations defined in Article 1 of Additional Protocol II to the Conventions referred to in point a),
c) * the state of war,
d) the state of emergency,
e) in the case of war crimes and military offenses, also the foreign deployment of the Hungarian Defence Forces;
11. official person:
a) the President of the Republic,
b) * a Member of Parliament, a nationality spokesperson and a Member of the European Parliament elected in Hungary,
c) a Constitutional Court judge,
d) * the Prime Minister, a minister, a state secretary, an administrative state secretary, a deputy state secretary and a lord-lieutenant,
e) a judge, a prosecutor and an arbitrator,
f) the Commissioner for Fundamental Rights and their deputy,
g) a notary public and a deputy notary public,
h) an independent judicial officer, a deputy independent judicial officer and a candidate independent judicial officer authorized to deliver documents,
i) a member of a local government and nationality self-government representative body,
j) * the commander of the Hungarian Defence Forces with jurisdiction over personnel, and the commander of a floating facility or aircraft, if authorized to apply the provisions relating to the investigating authority,
k) * a person performing public authority tasks or service at the Constitutional Court, the Sándor Palace, the Office of the National Assembly, the Office of the Commissioner for Fundamental Rights, the Hungarian National Bank, the State Audit Office, a court, a prosecution service, a central state administrative body, the Parliamentary Guard, a capital or county government office, a local government administrative body or a public body, whose activity belongs to the proper functioning of the body,
l) * a member of an electoral commission;
12. person performing public duties:
a) * a soldier performing service duties,
b) a person assigned to a civil defense organization and performing civil defense service,
c) a civilian guard during the performance of their activities as defined in the Act on civilian guards and the rules of civilian guard activities,
d) * a religious person and a member of a religious association who professionally performs religious ceremonies,
e) in court or other official proceedings, the defense counsel, the legal representative, the expert, and the delivery officer who does not qualify as an official person,
f) * a healthcare worker, another person in an employment relationship with a healthcare provider and a patient rights representative in the cases defined in the Act on healthcare,
g) a member of the state ambulance service and another organization authorized to provide rescue services in connection with rescue and patient transport,
h) * a member of a municipal and facility fire department, as well as a voluntary fire association during the performance of their firefighting and technical rescue tasks,
i) * in the case defined in the Act on national public education, a teacher, as well as an employee directly assisting in educational and teaching work, in the case defined in the Act on vocational training, an instructor, and in the case defined in the Act on national higher education, an instructor, teacher and scientific researcher of a higher education institution,
j) a person employed in a position defined in the Act on the protection of children and guardianship administration, as well as in the Act on social administration and social benefits, during the exercise of this activity,
k) a member of forestry professional staff and authorized forestry professional staff within the scope of their activities defined in the Act on forests, forest protection and forestry,
l) a professional hunter within the scope of their activities defined in the Act on the protection of game, game management and hunting,
m) * a fishery guard within the scope of their activities defined in the Act on fisheries management and fish protection,
n) a person performing enforcement tasks at a business organization operating public transport vehicles during this activity,
o) a person performing customer relations tasks at the universal postal service provider during this activity,
p) * a school guard within the scope of their activities defined in the Act on the Police,
q) * an armed security guard during the performance of their service,
r) * a person performing the monitoring of the proper operation of parking spaces and the proper use of parking spaces at an organization providing parking public services aimed at ensuring parking with road vehicles on national and local public roads, on private roads not closed to public traffic owned by the state or local government, as well as on squares, parks and other public areas, in accordance with the Act on road traffic, during this activity,
s) * a road inspector and an operator inspector within the scope of their activities defined in the Act on road traffic;
13. foreign official person:
a) * a person performing legislative, judicial, administrative or law enforcement tasks in a foreign state, or a person performing public authority tasks or service in a foreign state, including a person performing such tasks or service in a public body or in a state or municipal enterprise,
b) a person serving in an international organization established by an international treaty promulgated by law, whose activity belongs to the proper functioning of the organization,
c) a person elected to the general assembly or body of an international organization established by an international treaty promulgated by law, including a Member of the European Parliament elected abroad,
d) a member of an international court having jurisdiction over the territory of Hungary or its nationals, a person serving at the international court whose activity belongs to the proper functioning of the court;
14. relative:
a) a lineal relative and their spouse or cohabitant,
b) the adoptive parent and foster parent (including a cohabiting stepparent), the adopted child and foster child (including a cohabiting stepchild),
c) a sibling and the sibling's spouse or cohabitant,
d) the spouse, the cohabitant,
e) the lineal relative and sibling of the spouse or cohabitant;
15. information system: equipment ensuring the automatic processing, handling, storage, transmission of data, or the totality of such equipment in connection with each other;
16. damage: in the absence of a different provision of this Act, the decrease in value caused in property by the criminal offense;
17. pecuniary disadvantage: in the absence of a different provision of this Act, the damage caused in property and the lost pecuniary advantage;
18. * narcotic drug: a substance listed in the narcotics schedules 1 and 2 of Annex 1 of BM Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.) and in the psychotropic substances schedules 1 and 2 of Annex 2;
19. * cash substitute payment instrument means the cash substitute payment instrument defined in the Act on credit institutions and the negotiable order, the treasury card, the traveller's cheque, the voucher issued on the basis of law for the purpose of settling the consideration for goods or services of limited scope that may be given with or without tax payable by the payer, and the bill of exchange, provided that it is protected against copying, counterfeiting or unauthorized use by its design, coding or the signature on it;
20. electronic cash substitute payment instrument means, in addition to the cash substitute payment instrument defined in the Act on credit institutions, the treasury card and the electronic voucher issued on the basis of the authorization of the Act on personal income tax, provided that these are used through an information system;
21. public utility:
a) the public utility,
b) the public transport operation,
c) the electronic communications network,
d) the logistics, financial and information technology centers and operations operated for the purpose of performing the public interest tasks of the universal postal service provider,
e) a plant producing war material, military equipment, energy or raw materials for industrial use;
22. in great publicity shall also be understood to mean the commission of the criminal offense through publication in a press product, media service, reproduction or on an electronic communications network;
23. weapon prohibited by international treaty:
a) the asphyxiating, poisonous and other similar gases and bacteriological means of warfare referred to in the Protocol signed at Geneva on 17 June 1925 for the Prohibition of the Use in War of Asphyxiating, Poisonous or Other Gases, and of Bacteriological Methods of Warfare, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 20 of 1955,
b) the bacteriological (biological) and toxin weapons defined in Article 1 of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production and Stockpiling of Bacteriological (Biological) and Toxin Weapons and on their Destruction, adopted at the XXVI session of the United Nations on 10 December 1971, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 11 of 1975,
c) attached to the Convention on Prohibitions or Restrictions on the Use of Certain Conventional Weapons Which May be Deemed to be Excessively Injurious or to Have Indiscriminate Effects, signed at Geneva on 15 October 1980, promulgated by Decree-Law No. 2 of 1984,
ca) weapons causing injury by fragments which are not detectable by X-rays as defined in Protocol I,
cb) mines, remotely delivered mines, anti-personnel mines, booby-traps and other devices as defined in Article 2, paragraphs 1–5 of Amended Protocol II promulgated by Act CXXXIII of 1997,
cc) incendiary weapons as defined in Article 1, paragraph 1 of Protocol III,
cd) blinding laser weapons as defined in Article I of Protocol IV,
d) chemical weapons or chemical riot control agents as defined in Article II, paragraphs 1 and 7 of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Development, Production, Stockpiling and Use of Chemical Weapons and on their Destruction, signed in Paris on 13 January 1993, promulgated by Act CIV of 1997,
e) anti-personnel mines as defined in Article 2, paragraph 1 of the Convention on the Prohibition of the Use, Stockpiling, Production and Transfer of Anti-Personnel Mines and on their Destruction, adopted in Oslo on 18 September 1997, promulgated by Act X of 1998,
f) cluster munitions as defined in Article 2, paragraph 2 of the Convention on Cluster Munitions promulgated by Act XI of 2012, and explosive bomblets as defined in Article 2, paragraph 13;
24. * assembly: an assembly falling under the scope of the Act on the right of assembly;
24a. * public event: an event other than an assembly that is open to everyone under the same conditions;
25. prostitution means engaging in sexual acts for the purpose of regular profit;

27. sexual act: sexual intercourse and any act seriously offensive to decency which is suitable for or aimed at arousing, maintaining or satisfying sexual desire;
28. commits the crime on a commercial basis: a person who seeks to obtain regular profit through the commission of crimes of the same or similar nature;
29. * a person shall also be considered incapable of defence who, due to their situation or condition, is temporarily or permanently unable to offer resistance;
30. protected cultural property shall also mean cultural property declared protected;
31. * a recidivist is the perpetrator of an intentional crime who was previously sentenced to imprisonment to be executed for an intentional crime, and three years have not yet elapsed between the execution of the sentence or the termination of its enforceability and the commission of the new crime;
a) * a special recidivist is a recidivist who commits a crime of the same or similar nature on both occasions;
b) a multiple recidivist is a person who was sentenced to imprisonment to be executed as a recidivist prior to the commission of the intentional crime, and three years have not yet elapsed between the execution of the last sentence or the termination of its enforceability and the commission of the new crime punishable by imprisonment;
c) * a violent multiple recidivist is a multiple recidivist who commits a violent crime against a person on all three occasions;
32. * internationally protected person: a foreign official person who enjoys immunity based on international law under an international treaty applicable to them;
33. * new psychoactive substance: a substance specified in Annex 3 of Decree 78/2022. (XII. 28.) BM;
34. * cultural heritage element under foreign protection: under the Convention on Offences relating to Cultural Property of the Council of Europe, adopted in Nicosia on 19 May 2017, promulgated by Act CXXV of 2021
a) movable cultural heritage element classified, defined or designated in accordance with Article 2(2)(a), and
b) immovable cultural heritage element defined or designated in accordance with Article 2(2)(b);
35. * humanitarian operation: assistance activity carried out with the consent of the host country or with the authorisation of a competent international organisation with the substantive participation of the Defence Forces, aimed at eliminating and reducing the consequences of a situation caused by armed conflict, natural or civilisation disaster that seriously endangers or damages the lives, health, material assets, basic supply of people, or the natural environment;
36. * humanitarian activity in a foreign theatre of operations: participation of the Defence Forces in an area endangered by armed clashes between opposing parties, for the purpose of ensuring the lives, physical integrity, health and basic goods necessary for the sustenance of the civilian population;
37. * prohibited foreign support: support from abroad the acceptance or use of which is prohibited by the law on the operation and management of parties or by the law on electoral procedure;
38. * responsible person: the responsible person as defined in the law on civil organisations.
(2) Where this Act refers to a cohabitant, it shall also mean a registered cohabitant.
(3) * Where this Act refers to a member of the Defence Forces, theatre of operations, allied armed forces or peacekeeping, it shall mean the concepts defined in the law on national defence and the Hungarian Defence Forces.
(3a) * Where this Act refers to rank, it shall mean, in the case of a member of the Defence Forces, the rank held, and in the case of a professional member of the police, the Guard of the National Assembly, the penitentiary organisation, the professional disaster management body and the civilian national security services, the rank determined on the basis of the classification and pay grade of the service position held.
(4) Where this Act refers to data classified as top secret, secret, confidential or restricted, it shall mean national or foreign classified data as defined in the law on the protection of classified data.
(5) * For the purposes of this Act
a) third-country national shall mean the concept defined in the law on the general rules on the entry and residence of third-country nationals,
b) permit issued for the purpose of employment:
ba) the National Card,
bb) the Corporate Card issued under Section 4 of Act XC of 2023 on the general rules on the entry and residence of third-country nationals

## 8. § (2) paragraph b) or c),

bd) residence permit for employment purposes issued for the purpose of implementing an investment,
be) residence permit for employment purposes,
bf) guest worker residence permit,
bg) Hungarian Card,
bh) EU Blue Card,
bi) residence permit issued for the purpose of intra-corporate transfer (including the long-term mobility permit),
bj) residence permit for research purposes (including the long-term residence permit for researchers, as well as the short-term mobility certificate for researchers),
bk) residence permit for study purposes (including the student mobility residence permit, as well as the student mobility certificate),
bl) residence permit issued for the purpose of ensuring family reunification within the framework of a combined application procedure,
bm) residence permit issued for humanitarian purposes,
bn) residence permit for posting purposes.
(6) For the purposes of this Act, value, damage and pecuniary disadvantage
a) between fifty thousand and one and five hundred thousand forints is minor,
b) between five hundred thousand and one and five million forints is major,
c) between five million and one and fifty million forints is significant,
d) between fifty million and one and five hundred million forints is particularly large,
e) above five hundred million forints is particularly significant.
46

## 0. §

(1) For the purposes of Chapter XIV, a protected person is one who does not take a direct part in hostilities, in particular
a) a member of the armed forces who has laid down his or her arms and, having withdrawn from hostilities, has surrendered;
b) a person who has clearly become incapable of fighting as a result of illness, injury, capture or other cause, or has clearly withdrawn from hostilities;
c) personnel of a humanitarian aid or peacekeeping mission operating under the Charter of the United Nations, if they are entitled to the protection afforded to civilians or civilian objects by the international law of armed conflict;d) a civilian, to the extent and for as long as he or she does not take a direct part in hostilities.
(2) For the purposes of Chapter XXVI
a) a disciplinary matter shall be considered to be a procedure for which the law establishes the conduct constituting a disciplinary offence, the detailed rules of the procedure and the disciplinary penalties that may be imposed;
b) a civil matter shall also include a case pending before an arbitral tribunal.
46

## 1. §

(1) For the purposes of § 176–18

## 0., a narcotic drug is of small quantity if

a) its pure active substance content expressed in base form
aa) in the case of LSD, does not exceed 0.001 grams,
ab) in the case of psilocybin, does not exceed 0.1 grams,
ac) in the case of psilocin, does not exceed 0.2 grams,
ad) * in the case of amphetamine, methamphetamine, MDPV and alpha-PVP, does not exceed 0.5 grams,
ae) in the case of dihydrocodeine, does not exceed 0.8 grams,
af) in the case of heroin, does not exceed 0.6 grams,
ag) in the case of morphine, does not exceed 0.9 grams,
ah) in the case of ketamine, codeine, MDA, MDMA, N-ethyl-MDA (MDE), MBDB, 1-PEA, N-methyl-1-PEA, mCPP, methadone, 4-fluoroamphetamine and pethidine, does not exceed 1 gram,
ai) in the case of mephedrone, methylone and 4-MEC, does not exceed 1.5 grams,
aj) in the case of cocaine, does not exceed 2 grams,
ak) in the case of BZP, does not exceed 3 grams,
al) * in the case of pentedrone, does not exceed 0.4 grams,
b) in the case of GHB, its pure active substance content expressed in acid form does not exceed 7.5 grams,
c) in the case of tetrahydrocannabinol (THC), the pure and acid form THC content present together (total THC) does not exceed 6 grams,
d) * its pure active substance content
da) in the case of GBL, does not exceed 6.2 grams,
db) in the case of AB–CHMINACA, MDMB–CHMICA, AM–2201, AB–PINACA, AB–FUBINACA, and ADB–FUBINACA, does not exceed 0.05 grams.
(2) For the purposes of § 176–18

## 0.,

a narcotic drug is of small quantity if, in the case of cannabis plants, the number of plant individuals does not exceed five.
(3) For the purposes of § 176–18

## 0., the narcotic drug referred to in paragraphs (1)–(2)

a) is of significant quantity if it exceeds twenty times the upper limit of the small quantity specified for the given narcotic drug,
b) is of particularly significant quantity if it exceeds two hundred times the upper limit of the small quantity specified for the given narcotic drug.
(4) For the purposes of § 176–18

## 0.,

in the case of a narcotic drug not listed in paragraphs (1)–(2), the narcotic drug
a) is of small quantity if its pure active substance content does not exceed seven times the average effective dose for a non-habituated consumer,
b) is of significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds one hundred and forty times the average effective dose for a non-habituated consumer,
c) is of particularly significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds one thousand four hundred times the average effective dose for a non-habituated consumer.
(5) * For the purposes of §§ 184–184/C, a new psychoactive substance is of small quantity if its pure active substance content does not exceed the amount specified in the list in Annex 3 of Decree 78/2022 (XII. 28.) BM *
a) for the group of compounds under point 1, 0.5 grams,
b) for the group of compounds under point 2, 1.5 grams,
c) for the group of compounds under point 3, 2 grams,
d) for the group of compounds under point 4, 1 gram,
e) for the group of compounds under point 5, 0.1 grams,
f) for the substances under point 6, 2 grams.
(6) * For the purposes of §§ 184–184/C, a new psychoactive substance is of significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds twenty times the upper limit of the small quantity.
(7) * For the purposes of §§ 184/B and 184/C, a new psychoactive substance is of particularly significant quantity if its pure active substance content exceeds two hundred times the upper limit of the small quantity.
(8) * In the case of compounds occurring in salt form, pure active substance content shall mean the active substance content expressed in base form.
46

## 2. § (1) No criminal offence is committed if

a) negligent handling is committed causing pecuniary disadvantage not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,
b) abuse of social security, social or other welfare benefits is committed causing damage not exceeding fifty thousand forints.
(2) No criminal offence but a petty offence is committed if
a) vandalism and fraud are committed causing damage not exceeding fifty thousand forints,
b) * theft, embezzlement and misappropriation are committed for a value not exceeding fifty thousand forints,
c) breach of fiduciary duty is committed causing pecuniary disadvantage not exceeding fifty thousand forints,
d) infringement of copyright or related rights is committed
da) with respect to blank media fees or reprographic fees,
db) causing pecuniary disadvantage,
not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,
e) infringement of industrial property rights is committed causing pecuniary disadvantage not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,
f) * placing defective products on the market or imitation of a competitor is committed for a value not exceeding one hundred thousand forints,
g) * money laundering is committed for a value not exceeding fifty thousand forints, as specified in § 17

## 7. (1a) of Act II of 2012 on Petty Offences, Petty Offence Proceedings and the Petty Offence Registration System.

(3) * No criminal offence is committed, or a customs petty offence is committed, if the pecuniary disadvantage caused by budgetary fraud does not exceed five hundred thousand forints.
(4) * A criminal offence is committed if, in the case of petty offences committed by the same perpetrator within one year and adjudicated together,
a) * in the case of the same property petty offence, the value of the thing, the damage caused or the pecuniary disadvantage caused exceeds the amount specified in points a)–c) or g) of paragraph (2),
b) in the case of infringement of copyright or related rights, the blank media fee or reprographic fee exceeds the amount specified in point d) of paragraph (2),
c) in the case of infringement of industrial property rights, the pecuniary disadvantage exceeds the amount specified in point e) of paragraph (2),
d) in the case of placing defective products on the market or imitation of a competitor, the value of the product or goods exceeds the amount specified in point f) of paragraph (2),
e) in the case of a customs petty offence, the pecuniary disadvantage exceeds the amount specified in paragraph (3)
as a result of aggregation of values.
(5) * A criminal offence is committed in the case of petty offences of infringement of copyright or related rights committed by the same perpetrator and adjudicated together, if the individual acts infringe the copyright or related rights of another or others under the Copyright Act, causing pecuniary disadvantage exceeding the amount specified in point d) of paragraph (2).
46

## 3. §

This Act shall enter into force on 1 July 2013.
